**The last-pixel fault's candidates (2026-10-25, read from the runs
above; nothing new measured).** The owner's bar for divide 1 is lossless,
not statistical: a cure that makes the fault rarer isn't an answer until
its mechanism is known. What the runs already say:

- **Where:** one pixel, (55, 415), the last column and last row of its
  8 x 16 tile (`rp-mbx3d` `TILE_W`, `TILE_H`): the pixel the core writes out
  last. Its word's other pixel, (54, 415), was right (one pixel counted).
  Held on the second read 50 ms later: in memory, not in the reading.
  Kind 0, RGB565 Gouraud, no texture: no texture fetch in the scene.
- **The family:** GC2P placed divide 1's wrong words in rows 5-15 of the
  tile, rising to the last, in every tile order; GC2K's patch sat by a
  textured quad's corner; C71, G16-G21 found the display's reads past the
  safe region corrupting the core's writes at every clock. Every cure that
  worked changes what the memory is doing as a tile's write-out ends (an
  opaque hidden layer or ring makes the next tile start later; reversed
  order changes which tile follows; the screen inside the safe region
  takes the display's reads off the shared memory).
- **The value was never logged.** GC2AA's soak placed the pixel but not
  what it read (`gc2_orders` counts and places only), so the candidates
  below can't be told apart from GC2AA.

| Candidate | What it predicts the wrong pixel holds |
|---|---|
| (a) **the write lost**: the write-out's last beat dropped when another reader (the next tile's parameter fetch, the display) takes the memory in the same cycle | the picture the target held before: the scene rendered just before, or a poison fill |
| (b) **the tile buffer early**: the write-out starts before the shader's last pixel lands in the core's tile buffer | the same place in the tile drawn before (a neighbouring tile's pixel) |
| (c) **the write corrupted**: the data bus or the write's byte lanes past their timing at divide 1 | neither: a few bits off (the XOR), perhaps only one 16-bit lane |
| (d) **a late write from the render before** | the scene before's pixel, like (a); told apart by where it lands (a tile's last pixel in every tile, or only the last tile drawn) |

**A flaw found in GC2AD as built (2026-10-25):** its fourteen ways run each
base scene's ring way and full-layer way back to back, and the two draw
the same visible picture (kinds 0, 1 and 7 have no textured object, so the
ring way is the plain scene). A write lost in the second way leaves the
first way's identical pixel: the error can't be seen. GC2AA's one fault was
kind 0 with one full layer, exactly the way GC2AD would run blind to (a).

**Run GC2AD with the value logged and the target poisoned (built
2026-10-25, branch `gpu-gate`, variant `gc2ad2`, sha 1da60fab, not yet run).** The same
fourteen ways, cures, clock, order and length as GC2AD (sha 3084286b, kept
unchanged for the owner's choice); two additions, neither a cure:
(1) each render's target filled first by the 2D engine (finished before
the 3D core starts: no 2D work behind a render, R30) with a colour no
reference holds (`gc2::absent16` over the fourteen references, from
`gc2::POISON`), so any pixel the core doesn't write reads wrong whatever
ran before; (2) the first 40 wrong renders described pixel by pixel
(`Suspects`): the read value, the reference, the XOR, the word's other
pixel, the poison, the same place in the four tiles around, the render's
time. One fault then names its candidate: the poison is (a), a
neighbouring tile's pixel is (b), a few bits off is (c). Host tests: the
pixel reads at both halves of a word, the edges, a tile's last pixel and
its mates (`suspects_read_the_right_pixels`); the poison's choice
(`the_poison_is_in_no_reference`). `emu-gpubench` run GC2AD: the poison
line and every way right (the emulator overwrites the whole target).

**The faults described (the first 40 wrong renders: 39 FT2 both at once,
1 FT2 ring; the cap filled before GC2K's and kind 1's single faults, which
are placed but not described):**

- **None held the poison (0 of 40):** the pixel was written every time.
  Candidate (a), the write lost, is ruled out for these 40.
- **The same wrong value recurs exactly:** `0xFFBD` in 33 (by x 448-455, y
  45-47, over references `0xFFDD` and `0xFFDE`: one green step off the
  first), `0x31F3` in 4 (y 604-607, and each equal to the reference 16 rows
  above: the same place in the tile above), `0xFFFD` in 2, one far value
  in FT2's ring way (`0x07D4` for `0xF6A2` at (54, 607), close to the tile
  above's `0x0FD4`). A bus fault (c) would scatter bits; these are whole,
  repeated colours from the picture.
- **Where in the tile:** rows 12-15 only, row 15 (the last) in 29 of 40;
  all in one tile column (x 448-455) for the both-cures way. The word's
  other pixel always right.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** (1) **one full hidden layer,
reversed, with the clamped v shift drew all seven scenes right in every
render: 0 of 23,093** at 149.50 / 1 for 1-2% render time over none; the
ring alone left 3 (2 in textured scenes); (2) **the ring and the full
layer together made FT2 far worse** (160 of 3,299, against 0 and 1
apart): the cures don't add, they interact, so they can't be stacked
blind; (3) the fault is not a lost write and not random bits: the core
writes a real colour from elsewhere in the picture, in a tile's last rows,
often the pixel one tile above. That fits a pixel computed from the wrong
data (the tile's data or a texel fetched for another tile) more than a
memory write fault; it is not yet a mechanism.

**The margin: one full hidden layer reversed, the clamped v shift, all

The same wrong colours again (`0xFFBD` by x 448-455, y 45-47; `0x31F3` at
y 607; `0xFF02` at (31, 607) in rows order), never the poison, in a tile's
last rows. **The source search didn't find one place they come from:**
each is a common colour of the picture (`0xFFBD` 120 times), and its
nearest copy lies at a different offset each time ((+4, +4), (+3, +4),
(+1, +4) ...): no fixed neighbour, no fixed tile. One flaw in the run: the
fetch-off part's control at Windows Mobile's clock read "0 of 1 right"
while every reference before and after was right; most likely the
underrun flag latched as the fetch came back on (counted as not held),
fixed after the run (the flag settled and cleared before the controls).

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** (1) **the fault isn't the clock's
edge:** from 133.25 to 149.50 / 1 its rate stays 3-6%, while every
reference at Windows Mobile's clock (divide 2) is right: what matters is
divide 1 itself (the cores at the memory's clock), not how fast; (2)
**the display's reads take part:** with the fetch off the rate falls
about eightfold, not to zero, though the screen sits inside the safe
region; (3) **tile order doesn't matter** for this combination (rows
6.0%, reversed 6.3%); (4) the wrong colours are the picture's own, from no
fixed place: consistent with the core computing a tile's last rows from
data read at the wrong moment while another reader (the display, the
parameter fetch) holds the memory, more than with a misplaced write. The
mechanism is narrowed, not named. (5) R36's winning cure stays clean at
the owner's margin step.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** (1) **the fault needs the quad
drawn last, over the stack**: drawn first, 0; (2) **it is tied to exact
geometry, not to a simple rule:** the quad's top edge at y 40 (row 8 of
the tile row y 32-47) faults, at y 32 or y 44 it doesn't; its right
corner at x 436 or x 440 (the tile x 432-439) faults, at x 448 (the tile
beside the fault's column), 456 or 400 it doesn't; (3) the wrong pixels
stay in one tile, x 448-455, y 45-47, two tiles to the right of the
quad's corner tile, inside the ring's band: the core writes that tile's
last rows wrong when the quad's top-right corner sits in a particular
place two tiles before it in draw order. A geometry this specific points
at a corner case in the core's per-tile setup at divide 1, not at a rule
a scene can be designed around. **What this means for the cure:** one
full layer alone (no ring) has drawn 25,193 renders right over seven
scenes, but this fault depends on exact edge and corner positions, so
those seven scenes don't prove no other geometry trips it. The question
that decides Q3 is whether any quad corner, swept over the positions
around a tile, faults with one full layer alone.

**GC2AL's every-render fault read (2026-10-26, from gpubench-0112):**
FT2's texture has red = the texel's column, and every wrong pixel has red
one higher, green and blue right: the core sampled the texel one column
to the right, in whole screen columns 23 pixels apart (4 texels at 32
texels on 184 pixels), where a sample sits 1/46 of a texel from an edge
(the closest any comes at that ratio). So divide 1's texture coordinate
is off by at least 1/46 texel there, deterministically; GC2AK's textured
faults at 117.00 / 1 are the same kind. Design and research:
docs/design/divide1-texel-margin.md (the D3D9 half-pixel rule, 8-bit
sub-texel precision, per-vendor epsilons; ours: measure the error, then
draw point-sampled textures only at ratios whose margin 1 / (2p) clears
it, others scaled first by the 2D engine or the video scaler).

`gc2::best_phase`), at all 128 corner places, 30 rounds. Host test: the

(`gc2::margin`). `emu-gpubench` run GC2AM on the wm-clocks fork.

**GC2AM on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-26 12:15-12:33 PM, sha 29c7e4c1;
`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2AM.txt` through the 149.50 / 1 part, the run log
then full; 117.00 / 1 on the card, not yet read; 0 hung, every plan's
references and controls right).** At 149.50 / 1:

- **The phase sweep:** phases 0-5 (0 to 15 / 65,536 of the texture added
  to u) wrong in 3 of 3, **phases 6-30 (18 to 90) right in every render**.
  The wrong pick is always the next texel up, so a sample just before an
  edge is read past it: **divide 1's coordinate error is positive and
  under about 18 / 65,536 of the texture, 0.009 texel (about 2.3 of the
  8-bit steps)** at this rectangle.
- **The ratios at the phases designed** (1/(2p) texel, assuming samples at
  pixel corners): 1:1 wrong in 179 of 780, 2:1 in 466, 4:1 in 68, 1:2 in
  11. **The pattern refutes the corner assumption:** with samples at pixel
  centres those phases put 1:1, 2:1 and 4:1's samples exactly on texel
  edges (1:1: 1/2 + (m + 1/2) is a whole number) and 1:2's in the middle of
  a texel (1/2 + 2m + 1), which is what the counts show. **The core
  samples at pixel centres** (as D3D10 and OpenGL do), and the best phase
  is the one that puts (m + 1/2) / s + phase in the middles of the 1/p
  gaps: none at 1:1, 2:1 and 4:1, half a texel at 1:2.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the texel-pick fault is a small,
one-sided coordinate error (under 0.009 texel); with the phase chosen for
centre sampling, every ratio with p up to about 50 keeps its samples
clear of it (1/(2p) texel), 5.75:1 (p = 23) included with 1/46 texel,
over twice the error. The rule, corrected, is still lossless by
construction and now covers nearly every scale; GC2AM's 1:2's 11 wrong
renders (with samples mid-texel) are likely the tile race, not texel
picks. To prove next: the ratios and 5.75:1 at the corrected phase, at
149.50 and 117.00 / 1.

**GC2AM at 117.00 / 1 (the card log, `local/cardlog/gpubench-0113.txt`,
0 hung, every control right):** the phase sweep 0 wrong in all 31 phases;

mid-texel) is the tile race, not texel picks. Reading the card log

drawn one after the other to be side by side in a row**: every order that
does that (all three of GC2's old ones) faults at 4-13%; every order that

of the scene built to fault, at no cost in render time. That fits a
finished tile's write-out of its last rows colliding with the next tile's
work on the same lines of the target (the same SDRAM rows), which the
side-by-side neighbour shares and a tile below or elsewhere does not. 0
of 400 against a 5% rate is far beyond chance (0.95^400, about 1 in
10^9), but it is one scene: to prove next, the corner sweeps (flat and
textured, with the textured phase set for centre sampling) at 149.50 / 1
in the columns and checker orders, then an overnight soak.

core's per-row stepping down the tile's 16 rows drifting at divide 1
until, late in the tile, an edge, a colour or a texture coordinate
crosses a boundary. Leads (not decisions): edges and texture edges kept
out of a tile's last rows by placement (the OS controls its layout); a
setting for the tile's height or row stepping (GC2AP's header search, a
render-block search); the half-texel v shift restored beside the u

(16-21 ms, against Windows Mobile's 12.5 ms at 99.67 / 2 for the
Gouraud alone): no use as it stands. Leads (not decisions): the bits only
on objects with little overdraw (cost by area); a render-block register
search on the opaque path's faults for a setting that keeps its speed
(the fault generator and GC2AM's texel fault answer a bit in one render);
what bit 15 is.

**GC2AR on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-26, sha 9730367f;
`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2AR.txt` in the main checkout; 1,757 renders,
0 hung, the reference repeatable and the control at Windows Mobile's
clock right).** GC2AM's phase scene 0 (opaque, reversed, poisoned) at
149.50 / 1, each of the 1,696 bits of the render block's 53 words
(P2D_REGS, GC3T_REGS) flipped alone for one render; unflipped, 59 words
wrong in each of three renders at 4.35 ms.

- **No bit held 0 wrong**: no single bit of the render block cures the
  opaque path's fault (0 of 1,696).
- **The wrong count comes in steps of about 16 words** (43, 59, 74-75,
  90-91, 106, 122 hold 1,404 of the 1,600 renders outside the address
  words), and flips in words the renderer leaves at 0 move it across that
  whole range: one render's count can't rank a bit. The fault is several
  16-word units a render, and how many fail varies from render to render.
- **`0x664` bits 0-3 are the one consistent lead:** each gave 27-34 words
  wrong, the lowest of any flip (the only other 27 was `0x624` bit 31),
  and bits 1-3 added 7, 16 and 25 us to the render (4,359 / 4,368 / 4,377
  against 4,352), a cost that grows with the field's value, as a delay or
  count field's would.
- `0x61C` bit 1 and `0x644` bits 0-1 broke the whole picture (49,316 and
  152,470+ words): format and address bits, as expected.
- Every other flip's render held at 4.35 ms (4,346-4,377 us).

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the opaque path has no one-bit
cure in the render block; `0x664`'s low field is the lead to measure
properly (its values swept, several renders each, against an unused
word's flips as the control for the render-to-render spread), and the
16-word steps give the faulting unit's size.

**GC2AS on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-26, sha 2bc6550c;
`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2AS.txt` in the main checkout; 698 renders, 0 hung,
the reference repeatable and the control at Windows Mobile's clock
right).** GC2AR's set-up with `0x664` bits 0-3 at each value 0-15 in
rising order, 20 renders each, each beside one with the same value in
`0x6FC` (a word the renderer leaves at 0) instead:

| value | 0 | 1-9 | 10-15 |
|---|---|---|---|
| words wrong a render, `0x664` (most) | 43-121 (59-75) | 27-66 (27-50) | 11-42 (11) |
| the same in `0x6FC` | 43-108 (59-75) | 11-75 (27-50) | 11-34 (11) |
| render time | 4,349-4,355 us | 4,353-4,381 | 4,378-4,396 |

- **No value drew a render right** (0 of 320 in either word), and
  **`0x6FC` matched `0x664` at every value**, totals and times alike: the
  effect isn't `0x664`'s (GC2AR's lead was this, not the word).
- The fault fell in **steps** (59-75 at 0, mostly 27-50 from 1, mostly 11
  from 10) while the render time rose 1% (46 us) over the run.
- **The rising order can't tell a value from the run's drift**: GC2AT runs
  the values bit-reversed with a third render, no word written, beside
  each pair.

**GC2AT on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-26, sha f607d391;
`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2AT.txt` in the main checkout; 1,018 renders,
0 hung, the reference repeatable and the control at Windows Mobile's
clock right).** GC2AS with the values bit-reversed and a third render,
no word written, beside each pair (words wrong in 20 renders):

| `0x664` | 0 | 1-3 | 4-7 | 8-9 | 10-15 | nothing written (every step) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| total | 1,423 | 753-968 | 825-988 | 644-786 | 243-348 | 1,427-1,656 |
| render time | 4,352 us | 4,354-4,366 | 4,365-4,375 | 4,374-4,380 | 4,378-4,396 | 4,349-4,356 |

- **Not drift: the value.** The renders with nothing written stayed at
  about 75 words wrong all run; with the value, the fault fell to about 13
  a render at 12-15 (83% less), and the render took about 2.8 us more a
  step (40 us at 15, under 1%). No value drew a render right (0 of 320).
- **The `0x6FC` column was not a control** in GC2AS or GC2AT: a `Rig::over`
  override writes its words and never puts others back, and the loop
  cleared `0x664` only after both renders, so every `0x6FC` render still
  had `0x664` at the same value (GC2AR's own times show `0x6FC`'s bits
  slowing nothing, `0x664`'s bits 1-3 the only ones that do). GC2AU
  repeats the control with `0x664` cleared first.
- **Reading it (results, not decisions):** `0x664` bits 0-3 behave as a
  wait the core takes (time in steps, the fault falling with it),
  consistent with GC2T's race (the core starting the next tile before the
  last rows are written out); the field's top, 15, leaves about 13 words.

**GC2AU on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-26, sha f5019e6a;
`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2AU.txt` in the main checkout; 650 renders, 0 hung,
the reference repeatable and the control at Windows Mobile's clock
right).** GC2AT's set-up at 149.50 / 1, words wrong a render:

- **The clean control:** `0x664` = 15 245 in 20 renders (12 a render,
  4,389-4,395 us); `0x6FC` = 15 with `0x664` cleared 1,450 (4,349-4,354);
  nothing written 1,517 (4,350-4,355). **`0x664`'s effect is its own.**
- **None of bits 4-31 extends it:** `0x664` = 15 with each set as well,
  88-142 in 8 renders (11-18 a render), every one at 4,391-4,398 us: the
  field is bits 0-3, its top 15.
- **The tile order decides what the timer does** (20 renders each,
  nothing set / `0x664` = 15): reversed 1,518 / 316; rows 959 / 1,555;
  columns 3,139 / 2,127; columns reversed 1,504 / 294; checker 1,582 /
  1,791; scrambled 1,116 / 1,254; interleaved 1,934 / 1,283. It helps
  most in the two reversed orders (about 80% less) and hurts in rows.
- No way drew a render right.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the timer moves the race rather
than simply lengthening a wait (its effect turns with the order the
tiles are drawn in); about 15 words a render remain at the best
combination (reversed or columns reversed with `0x664` = 15). Next: where
those words are (tile, row, value), and whether it is the same few tiles
every render, which decides between a targeted redo and a placement rule.

**GC2AV on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-26, sha 1e24bec8;
`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2AV.txt` in the main checkout; 98 renders, 0 hung,
the reference repeatable and the control at Windows Mobile's clock
right).** Every wrong word placed (tiles 8 x 16, 60 x 40 of them), 20
renders a way, reversed, 149.50 / 1:

- **`0x664` = 15:** 11 words in 16 of 20 renders, 18-27 in the rest, **all
  in one or two pixel columns**: tile (8,21) at x 66, rows 0-10, in every
  render (220 words over the 20), tile (5,21) at x 42 in a few, (22,15) in
  one. Every wrong pixel holds its right-hand neighbour's value (`0x3D94`
  read as `0x4594`: the next texel along u), never anything else.
- **`0x664` = 0:** 43-106 a render over 12 tiles, (5,21) 320, (11,19)
  288, (14,18) 272, (8,21) 220, (16,17) 195, (22,14) 112, the rest 3-48;
  the rows in the tile even down to row 10 (101-103), fewer in 11-15
  (68-81).
- **Reading it (results, not decisions):** what remains is GC2AM's texel
  pick, one texel off along u on whole pixel columns, on the scene built
  to put pixel centres on texel edges (phase 0), not a lost write; the
  timer takes it from 12 tiles to one column of one tile. Next: the
  timer on GC2AM's other phases and ratios, the placements a game makes.

**GC2AW on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-26, sha eeaf6c24;
`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2AW.txt` in the main checkout; 674 renders, 0 hung,
every reference repeatable and every control at Windows Mobile's clock
right).** GC2AM's phase sweep (31 phases of 3 / 65,536 of the texture, 5
rounds, reversed) at 149.50 / 1, `0x664` at 0 then at 15 for each whole
plan:

| | phases wrong (5 of 5 each) | phases right | wrong pixels a render | fastest render |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| timer 0 | 0-5 | 6-30 | 59-91 | 4,347-4,353 us |
| timer 15 | 0-5 | 6-30 | 11-18 | 4,389-4,394 us |

- **The timer leaves the coordinate error's width as it was** (the same
  six phases, under 18 / 65,536 of the texture, 0.009 texel), and cuts
  the pixels that cross it by 80% (the rest all the next texel along u,
  `+1`, none wrapped or other).
- Windows Mobile's clock with the timer at 15: the references 12,990-12,994
  us against 12,835-12,838 (1.2% slower), right.
- **Reading it (results, not decisions):** the texel pick is a small
  one-sided error the timer doesn't remove; a sample kept more than 0.009

  is GC2AM's placement rule. Next (GC2AX): whether the timer ends the

**GC2AX on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-26, sha 268252cf;
`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2AX.txt` in the main checkout; 5,814 renders,
0 hung, every reference repeatable and every control at Windows Mobile's
clock right).** GC2AQ's three plans at 149.50 / 1, reversed, poisoned,
`0x664` at 0 then at 15 for each whole plan (wrong renders):

| | fault generator (400) | flat sweep (1,150) | textured sweep (1,100) |
|---|---|---|---|
| timer 0 | 15 | 40 | 25 |
| timer 15 | 21 | 58 | 50 |

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the timer is no cure for the
untextured tile-row faults or the corner sweeps (as many wrong or more,
one textured scene 29 of 50 with it against 0); its 80% on GC2AM's phase
scene is that scene's alone. GC2AQ's punch-through path stays the only
setting found that ends every divide-1 fault class (at 3.7-4.5x the
render time on these scenes).

**What would prove the mechanism, not only name it (proposed, not built):**
if tonight's soak names (a), a short daytime run makes the fault common by
raising the reads that would collide with a tile's last write: kind 0 at
149.50 / 1, poisoned, with (i) the display's fetch paused, (ii) as now,
(iii) the CPU reading the 2700G's memory throughout, (iv) more objects a
tile (more parameter reads), each rate against the others. A lost write
whose rate follows the competing reads is the mechanism, and its cure is
then designed against it (reads kept off the memory while a tile's
write-out ends), not found by trial.

**Run GC2AY built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_BLOCK=1`, variant
`gc2ay`, 2026-10-26): the 3D core's other register block.** GC2AR searched
the render block (`0x600`-`0x6FC`) bit by bit and found no cure; the
punch-through path's cure costs 3.7-4.5x. This searches the TA and
parameter-manager block, `0x804`-`0x87C`, for a setting that ends the
opaque path's fault and keeps its speed.

- **What Dell's driver does (a finding):** his GLES writes nine of these
  words per scene (`0x840`-`0x85C`, the TA table) and the manager's
  (`0x824`-`0x83C`) once; the rest are 0 under Windows Mobile at both
  divides (rp-wmprobe v3). He never tried a value outside his table.
- **The textbook search** flips each bit once and keeps a render with no
  wrong word. On this scene that's useless: GC2AJ's flat corner scene 20
  draws wrong in 26 of 50 renders with nothing changed, so one clean
  render means nothing (and GC2AR saw a render's count wander by whole
  16-word units).
- **Ours:** one render a bit can't tell, so each bit gets four renders;
  with nothing changed all four come out clean only about 6% of the time.
  A bit that stays clean in all four is held for twelve more (0.5^16:
  under 1 in 60,000 by chance). Every bit's count of wrong renders out of
  four is logged (one line a word), so a bit that *lowers* the rate shows
  even if it doesn't end the fault. Sixteen unflipped renders give the
  scene's rate on the night, and each candidate's render time is logged
  beside the unflipped one's (a cure that costs the speed is no cure).
  Each word is read first: rp-wmprobe v3 and G9 read the whole block
  without a freeze. Each bit goes back to the word's value after its
  renders (GC2AS's and GC2AT's lesson: `Rig::over` puts nothing back).
- **Left out, and why:** the words Dell writes as 1 to start something
  (`0x800` the TA, `0x808`, `0x814`, `0x818`, `0x81C`, `0x834`, `0x838` the
  manager's set-up): a flip there starts work mid-render instead of
  changing a mode. Also left out: the core's control words (`0x080` the
  reset, `0x12C`-`0x134` status and interrupts) and `0x700`-`0x7FC` (never
  read under Dell, so reading or writing it is unproven), and the system
  block's memory controller (GC2T: loosening its timings made the fault
  worse). The next search, if this one finds nothing, is `PFCEIL` (the
  system block's prefetch ceiling), written once around a whole set of
  renders rather than per render.
- 25 words, 800 bits, about 3,300 renders: about 15 minutes, unattended.

**GC2AY on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-26 about 8:16-8:35 PM, image of
3bee4e0d; `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2AY.txt`, card log `gpubench-0126`;
3,733 renders, 0 hung, the reference repeatable, the control at Windows
Mobile's clock after right).** Scene 20's reference 13.5 ms at Windows
Mobile's clock; at 149.50 / 1, 4.50 ms a render (no flip changed it).
The words as found: `0x824` `0xFC000`, `0x828` `0x101`, `0x82C` `0x1FF`,
`0x83C` `0x100000`, `0x85C` `0x8000`, every other 0.

- **No bit cures it.** Six bits stayed clean for 16 renders (`0x804`
  bits 5, 8, 14; `0x80C` bit 30; `0x820` bit 0; `0x858` bit 9), all in
  the run's first words, where the fault was rare. At that rate (about
  17% of renders wrong) a bit with no effect stays clean 16 times about
  5% of the time, and words 0-1 hold 64 bits, so about 3 chance hits
  were expected there. No bit stands out from its neighbours.
- **The finding: the fault rate climbs through the run**, whatever is
  flipped. The 16 unflipped renders first: 1 wrong (6%). Each word's
  128 renders in run order: 19%, 16%, 29%, 27%, 39%, 34%, 35%, 25%,
  37%, 38%, 33%, 39%, 27%, 38%, 38%, 32%, 48%, 40%, 37%, 40%, 49%, 40%,
  43%, 44%, 49%. Over about 19 minutes at 149.50 / 1 the same scene
  went from about 1 render in 16 wrong to about 1 in 2. GC2AJ's "26 of
  50" was measured late in a long run, so the rate isn't a constant of
  the scene.
- **The test's own flaw (said plainly):** its control renders all came
  first, not interleaved with the flips as the owner's robustness rules
  ask, so the drift could only show up afterwards. Every search from now
  on puts an unflipped render beside each flipped one.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** something that builds up while
the core runs at 149.50 / 1 widens the fault. Heat is the likely
candidate, since the chip warms at the higher clock and a timing margin
shrinks as silicon warms; an effect that accumulates in the core or
memory is the other. If it's heat, divide 1's fault is a timing margin,
the cures found so far are working around it, and the fix is designed
for that margin (cooler render patterns, the clock stepped down by
temperature, or whatever keeps the slow path inside its margin), not
found by flipping bits. Two earlier runs fit this: GC2J-era kind 0's
wrong rate "warmed up over the first ~15 minutes ... and then held", and
GC2V/GC2Y's two-hour soak didn't grow (called "not heat" then, but it
was measured after the warm-up a rise-then-hold would finish). A rise
from cold that levels off is what a heat balance looks like, so the
earlier "not heat" isn't proof against it. Next (proposed, GC2AZ): (1) the fault rate over
time with nothing flipped: 20 minutes at 149.50 / 1, then 10 minutes
idle at Windows Mobile's clock, then 149.50 / 1 again (a rate that
resets after the pause and climbs again is heat, or something cleared
by idling; one that stays up accumulates); (2) the six held bits again,
each render beside an unflipped one, 40 pairs each.

**Run GC2AZ built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_HEAT=1`, variant
`gc2az`, 2026-10-26): divide 1's fault rate over time.** Scene 20 of
GC2AJ's flat sweep, reversed, every target poisoned, nothing flipped.

- **The usual way** (ours until now, and the textbook's) runs a fixed
  number of renders and reports one rate, assuming the rate is a
  property of the setting. GC2AY showed it moving fourfold within one
  run, so a single rate hides the thing that matters.
- **Ours:** the rate as a time series, one line a minute (renders, wrong
  renders, median render time), through four phases on one image: (1) 20
  minutes at 149.50 / 1 from the loader's idle (the device has sat at
  Windows Mobile's clock since GC2AY); (2) 10 minutes idle at Windows
  Mobile's clock, the 3D core not drawing; (3) 20 minutes at 149.50 / 1
  again; (4) still warm, GC2AY's six clean bits rechecked, each in 40
  pairs of one unflipped and one flipped render, which goes first
  alternating pair by pair, so drift hits both sides equally.
- **What each outcome means:** phase 3 starting low and climbing again
  as phase 1 did is a balance that resets when the chip idles (heat, the
  likely one); phase 3 starting where phase 1 ended is a build-up that
  idling doesn't clear; a flat rate in both is that GC2AY's climb was
  something else in that run. A bit whose flipped renders are wrong
  clearly less often than its paired controls lowers the rate; one equal
  to them was chance.
- The X51v has no temperature sensor the loader can read (none found in
  the PXA27x or the 2700G), so time and the idle pause stand in for
  temperature here; a reading by hand (the owner's finger on the back)
  isn't asked for. **Corrected after the run:** the touch controller's
  `TEMP0` input reads steadily (878-882, entropy.md): a thermometer on
  the board, of the touch chip's own die, not the 2700G's.
- About 52 minutes (20 + 10 + 20, and the pairs about 2), unattended.

**GC2AZ on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-26 about 8:56-9:50 PM, image of
f6682a2f; `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2AZ.txt`, card log `gpubench-0127`;
14,406 renders, 0 hung, the reference repeatable, the control at Windows
Mobile's clock after right).** Wrong renders a minute (percent):

| minute | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 8 | 10 | 12 | 14 | 16 | 18 | 20 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| hot, from the loader's idle | 19 | 23 | 29 | 33 | 34 | 31 | 30 | 36 | 38 | 46 | 41 | 53 | 52 |
| hot again, after 10 min idle | 22 | 32 | 41 | 41 | 50 | 53 | 48 | 50 | 55 | 49 | 50 | 53 | 55 |

- **The rate falls after the pause and climbs again**: 52% at the end of
  the first hot phase, 22% in the first minute after the pause, then
  back to 50% by minute 5 (it took 18 minutes the first time). The pause
  was two changes at once, 10 minutes without drawing **and** the clock
  put back to Windows Mobile's and set to 149.50 / 1 again (the lock
  count read 10 the first time, 0 the second), so the fall can't be
  credited to cooling.
- **The core was busy about 3% of the time**: about 420 renders a minute
  is 143 ms a cycle, of which the 3D core drew 4.5 ms; the rest was the
  CPU poisoning and checking the picture over the bus. Heat from drawing
  is an unlikely driver at that share.
- **The render time didn't move**: 4,499-4,500 us every minute, cold or
  hot. The core doesn't slow down as it warms; it gets more pixels wrong
  at the same speed. (The renders a minute fall from 420 to 320 only
  because each wrong render's check takes longer.)
- **The start isn't clean either**: even the first minute had 19%
  wrong. Whatever drives the climb widens the fault; it doesn't create
  it.
- **GC2AY's six bits were chance**: flipped against its interleaved
  controls, wrong in 19/20, 23/20, 25/27, 24/26, 21/11 and 16/17 of 40.
  None lowers the rate (`0x820` bit 0 is worse, which is within chance
  for 40 pairs).

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** something that builds up over
minutes at 149.50 / 1 widens the fault, and the pause (no drawing, with the
clock set again) brings it most of the way back down. First read as heat;
**the owner pushed back** ("heat would not be so uniform"), and with the
core busy 3% of the time and the pause confounded, heat is one candidate
of three: (1) a state that builds up while the clock stays at 149.50 and
is cleared by setting the clock again (the PLL's lock, the local memory's
refresh or timing state, a buffer in the 2700G); (2) the supply sagging
under the CPU's and the bus's long load and recovering at rest; (3) heat,
of the board or the PXA from the CPU's checking more than of the 3D core.
Until one is shown, any cure or clock is judged late in a long run, not
from the first minutes (GC2AJ's 26 of 50 was measured late). GC2BA
separates them.

**Run GC2BA built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_CAUSE=1`, variant
`gc2ba`, 2026-10-26): which of the three drives the climb.** Scene 20,
reversed, poisoned, nothing flipped, at 149.50 / 1 throughout, four
phases of 10 minutes in one-minute slices (renders, wrong, mean render
time, and the touch controller's `TEMP0`, 64 conversions averaged):

- **A, drawing:** the climb as in GC2AZ (and `TEMP0`'s change with it).
- **B, the clock put back and set again at once, then drawing:** if the
  rate drops at B's first minute, setting the clock clears whatever
  builds up (candidate 1); a few milliseconds can't cool anything.
- **C, not drawing for 10 minutes, the clock left at 149.50 / 1:** the
  CPU and the bus are idle too, so heat and the supply both recover,
  while a state tied to the clock's setting would not.
- **D, drawing:** if D starts low, resting cleared it without a clock
  change (heat or supply: `TEMP0` tells which follows the rate); if D
  starts where C began, resting does nothing and only setting the clock
  does.
- **What it can't tell:** heat from a supply sag, when both follow
  `TEMP0`. That's the owner's charger-in, charger-out run (D in the reply
  of 2026-10-26), only if A-D leave it open.
- **What changes on the board:** the touch controller's chip select
  (GPIO24) is set to a plain output, as rp-noise and rp-today already
  do (no new board bit).
- **What the old way was:** GC2AZ changed two things in one pause and
  had no thermometer. Ours changes one thing a phase and logs a
  temperature beside each minute's rate.
- About 42 minutes, unattended.

## Divide 1 read whole: the mechanism as the evidence stands (2026-10-26 night)

Every divide-1 run from G21 to GC2AZ read together (the owner, after the
switch to a stronger model: "reason out the results and design the next
test that will lead to a fix"). What is proven, what is inferred, and
what each fix would rest on.

**1. The fault is in the 3D core, driven by the core's own clock.**
- 149.50 / 2 (the memory at 149.50 MHz, the core at 74.75) drew every
  corner place and the fault generator right: 0 of 4,900 (GC2AF, GC2AK).
  74.75 / 1: 0 of 400. 99.67 / 1 held 20 minutes (GC2I). From 117 / 1
  up it faults, rising with the clock over the corner sweep (GC2AK: 0,
  6, 3, 21 of 2,300 at 117, 133.25, 139.75, 149.50). So the memory's
  clock is innocent and the core's clock above about 100 MHz is the
  cause. (The SDRAM's own edge is 152.75-156, 2% above 149.50: a
  separate limit on how far the clock can go, not this fault.)
- Every reader outside the core taken away leaves it: the display's
  fetch, the CPU's status reads, the texture's place, the parameter
  memory's place (GC2AE-GC2AG); no texture at all leaves it (GC2AG).
- Nothing written to the chip cures it: all 1,696 render-block bits
  (GC2Q, GC2AR), all 800 TA-block bits (GC2AY), 134 object-header bits
  (GC2AP) bar the two that change the path (below).

**2. The wrong pixels are computed values slightly off, late in a
tile, never lost writes and never random bits.** The poison never
showed (0 of 40 described in GC2AD, every run after); the XORs are
whole colour steps, not scattered bits. Four forms, one shape: the
Gouraud shade one step off (GC2AD), the next texel along u (GC2AM,
GC2AV), an edge drawn one row too far (GC2AO), the shade 16 rows up.
All in rows 5-15 of the 8 x 16 tile, rising to row 15 (GC2P). A tile is
shaded row by row from per-object setup values (a start and a delta
each for colour, u, v and the edges); a small error in a delta or a
dropped carry grows with the rows walked, so it shows last in the
tile. The deterministic texel error (GC2AM: +0.009 texel at 149.50 /
1, every render, smaller at 117 / 1, one-sided) is the clearest case:
the low bits of the u iterator lost the same way every time, which is
what one adder path missing its setup time at the top clock looks
like, not what a memory fault looks like.

**3. The path matters: the deferred-shading path fails, the
punch-through path doesn't.** TSP bit 14 (punch-through) or bit 15 on
every object: 0 of 7,150 across every fault class (GC2AQ), at 3.7-4.5x
the time on these scenes (it shades every object's pixels, not only
the visible ones). The opaque path does visibility first and shades
each visible pixel afterwards from the object's setup values; that is
the path with the iterators in (2). Which half of it fails (the
per-tile setup from parameter memory, or the per-row stepping) is not
separated yet.

**4. The memory schedule decides where it strikes; it is not the
cause.** Slower SDRAM timings made it 18x worse (GC2T: 4 to 72 of 100),
the display's fetch off 2-8x better (GC2AE, GC2AF), `0x664`'s wait 80%
better on one scene and worse in rows order (GC2AU, GC2AX), tile orders
that never draw neighbours in a row 0 of 400 on one scene (GC2AN) yet
1.5% on the sweep (GC2AO), and the rate depends on exact edge places
(GC2AI). A path inside the core shouldn't care about any of that unless
the stalls the memory causes are what exercise it: a pipeline that
holds and resumes on every memory wait, with the marginal path on the
hold-and-resume. Everything in this list changes when and how often
the core waits for memory, and none of it is needed at divide 2, where
the memory is always ahead of a core at half its clock.

**5. Time: the schedule is fixed, the failure probability isn't.** The
strongest fact in GC2AY and GC2AZ is one that was passed over: the
render time was 4,499-4,501 us in every minute of 50, cold or warm,
while the wrong rate went 19% to 55%. So nothing in what the core does
per render changes with time (the same stalls, the same work); what
changes is the chance that a fixed set of events fails. That is the
signature of a timing margin crossed more often as the silicon's delay
grows, which temperature does (delay grows a few tenths of a percent
per degree) and a sagging supply also would. It is not the signature
of a state building up (that would change the schedule or the time),
and the owner's objection to heat ("would not be so uniform") is
answered by what the chip was doing: between renders the CPU read and
poisoned 600 KiB through the 2700G's memory controller nonstop, so the
whole die was driven hard the whole time, and a small die under a PDA's
shell warming over 15-20 minutes to a plateau is what GC2K (rose 15
minutes, then held) and GC2Y (flat over two hours, measured after the
rise) show. Heat against supply is still open; GC2BA's phases and the
touch chip's `TEMP0` tell heat from a cleared state, and the owner's
charger-in / charger-out run tells heat from supply.

**6. What this means for a fix.** The 2700G's core supply has no
software control on the X51v (the three voltage pins are the PXA's:
clocks.md; 2700g-full-potential.md), so the levers that exist for a
timing-margin fault are: the clock, the path, the schedule, and the
temperature. Each is a design we can build and prove, not a bit to
find:
- **(A) The schedule: a stall-free memory layout at divide 1.** If (4)
  holds, every fault needs a memory wait at the wrong moment. The waits
  come from the target, the parameter lists and the textures sharing
  SDRAM banks and rows, and from the display's reads. Our layout is
  ours to choose: target, parameters and textures each in their own
  bank, the display band inside the on-die region, `0x664`'s wait set,
  and an order that never draws neighbours. The measurement that
  decides it: the fault rate against the render time (the stall count's
  proxy) over schedule ways at a fixed warm temperature. A rate that
  tracks the stalls to zero is the fix at full speed.
- **(B) The path, where it is cheap:** punch-through on objects with
  little overdraw, measured on a game-like scene, proven warm.
- **(C) Temperature:** if GC2BA names heat, the lossless rule is a
  clock keyed to `TEMP0` (149.50 / 1 while cool, the next proven step
  when warm), each step proven at its temperature; the touch chip's
  thermometer as the GPU's. Dell never had a divide-1 fast mode to
  manage; this uses the board's parts together.
- **(D) The honest warm ceiling:** the hot ladder, the fastest step
  with 0 wrong at the plateau, with no cure: today's shipping clock
  if (A) and (C) fall short.

**7. The test order.** GC2BA first (built; 42 minutes): it settles (5)
and so whether (C) is on the table and whether every later measurement
must be made warm. Then GC2BB, the schedule test for (A), at the warm
plateau with controls interleaved render by render: the baseline; the
display's fetch off; `0x664` = 15; the target, the parameters and the
texture each in a separate SDRAM bank; the lot together; and the
punch-through path beside them as the known-clean reference. Each way's
render time and wrong rate; a rate that falls with the render time's
stall share is (A)'s proof and its slope says how far it goes.

**Run GC2BB built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_ONE=1`, variant
`gc2bb`, 2026-10-26): one run for the fix.** The owner: "build anything
you need to be able to solve this issue into one test". Scene 20,
reversed, poisoned, five parts, `TEMP0` logged at each, about 60 minutes
unattended:

1. **GC2BA's phases** A-D (heat against a cleared state, 40 minutes).
2. **The stall ways, warm**, each in 300 pairs with the baseline, which
   goes first on alternate pairs so drift lands on both sides: the
   display's fetch off; `0x664` = 15; **the tile lists on the die**
   (a second renderer with its parameter memory in the 36 KiB left of
   G19's safe region, never tried: GC2AG moved them SDRAM to SDRAM);
   all three at once; the punch-through path as the clean reference.
   Wrong and mean render time each side: the fault rate against the
   stalls. A rate that tracks the time to zero is fix (A).
3. **The ladder, warm**: 146.25, 143.00, 139.75, 133.25, 117.00 / 1, the
   baseline paired with the all-three way, 150 pairs a step: the
   shipping clock at the plateau (D), and whether (A) raises it.
4. **Punch-through's cost at low overdraw**: the scene without its
   hidden layer (the same picture; a third of the overdraw), opaque
   paired with punch-through, 300 pairs: what a game would pay (B).
5. **The texel error warm**: GC2AM's phases 0-11 (cold: 0-5 wrong, 6 on
   right) at 5 renders each, their references at Windows Mobile's
   clock: whether the coordinate error's edge moves with heat, which
   sets the placement rule's margin.

The old way measured one thing a run and from cold; this measures the
fix's every lever on one warm chip, paired so each answer stands on its
own. What it can't do: tell heat from supply (the owner's charger run)
or raise the 2700G's voltage (no control exists).

**GC2BB on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-26 about 11:00-11:44 PM, image of
5d725965; `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2BB.txt`, card log `gpubench-0128`;
20,360 renders, 0 hung, every reference repeatable, the on-die
renderer's picture the reference's, the control at Windows Mobile's
clock after right).** The device had sat idle 70 minutes at Windows
Mobile's clock first. `TEMP0` is the touch chip's diode (lower is
warmer; about 3-4 counts a degree).

**Part 1, the phases** (wrong renders a minute of about 480, `TEMP0`):

| | min 1 | 3 | 5 | 7 | 10 | TEMP0 start to end |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A, drawing from idle | 3% | 5% | 5% | 5% | 6% | 879 to 872 |
| B, the clock set again at once | 6% | 6% | 8% | 8% | 7% | 872 to 871 |
| C, not drawing, clock left at 149.50 / 1 | - | - | - | - | - | 871 to 872 |
| D, drawing | 6% | 4% | 6% | 6% | 7% | 872 to 871 |

- **Setting the clock again does nothing** (B carried on from A's rate):
  no state is cleared by it. GC2AZ's fall after its pause was the
  cooling, not the clock.
- **Ten minutes idle at 149.50 / 1 didn't cool the chip** (`TEMP0`
  flat) **and didn't lower the rate** (D began where B ended). GC2AZ's
  pause, at Windows Mobile's clock, did both. A 2700G clocked at 149.50
  stays warm idle.
- **The rate followed the thermometer**: both rose over A's first
  minutes and then held; the whole run sat at 3-8% where GC2AZ's second
  half sat at 50%, after 70 minutes' rest against GC2AZ's 20 after
  GC2AY: the device's body, not only the die, carries the heat, and
  takes an hour or more to shed it. Supply against heat is still not
  separated (the owner's charger run); every other candidate is out.
- The render time: 4,486 us in every minute.

**Part 2, the stall ways, warm, 300 pairs each beside the baseline:**

| Way | wrong of 300 | its baseline beside it | render time |
|---|---|---|---|
| baseline against baseline | 20 | 15 | 4,486 us |
| **the display's fetch off** | **0** | 15 | 4,485 |
| `0x664` = 15 | 13 | 17 | 4,486 |
| the tile lists on the die | 16 | 29 | 4,486 |
| **all three** | **0** | 16 | 4,484 |
| punch-through (see the flaw) | 20 | 14 | 4,486 |

- **The display's reads are the trigger on this scene: with the fetch
  off, 0 of 300 at full speed, warm, against 15 beside it** (by chance
  about 1 in 5 x 10^7). The scene draws into the on-die region and the
  display scans the same region; the core's tile write-out waits on the
  display's read, and that wait is the moment the marginal path fails.
  GC2AE/GC2AF's ring scene kept 1.6% with the fetch off: that scene has
  another stall (its parameter lists) feeding the same path.
- The per-tile wait does little alone here. **The "lists on the die"
  row and the "all three" row's baseline are void (audit C91, found
  2026-10-27):** the two renderers each kept a shadow of the core's
  registers, so from the first switch both sides of those pairs drew the
  on-die lists. "All three"'s 0 of 300 stands as a result of the fetch
  off (its own row), not of the lists. GC2BC measures the lists on the
  die properly (`core_taken` at every switch).
- **Flaw (audit C91):** the punch-through way drew at the opaque
  path's time (4,486 us; GC2AQ's bit 14 took 20 ms on these scenes): it
  ran after the on-die renderer, whose frame left the core on the on-die
  lists, so both of its sides drew those (the stale shadow, found on
  GC2BC's emulator check). Its rows in parts 2 and 4 say nothing; GC2BC
  measured it right (0 of 50 at 20.2 ms).

**Part 3, the ladder, warm, 150 pairs a step (baseline / all three):**
146.25 / 1 **0 / 0** (4,586 us), 143.00 0 / 0 (4,691), 139.75 0 / 0
(4,800), 133.25 0 / 0 (5,034), 117.00 0 / 0 (5,734). On this scene at
this run's warmth, 146.25 / 1 is clean in 300; 0 of 300 allows up to
about 1% (95%).

**Part 4, low overdraw:** the scene without its hidden layer drew at the
same 4,486 us (the layer costs nothing), opaque 17 of 300; its
punch-through side is void (the flaw).

**Part 5, the texel phases warm:** phases 0-11 at 5 renders: only phase
2 wrong once; **phases 0-5, wrong in every render in GC2AM and GC2AW,
were right in 29 of 30 here.** The deterministic coordinate error has
moved: smaller on this cooler run than on the afternoon's warm one. So
it too is a margin that temperature sets, and the placement rule's
0.009 texel is a warm number, to be re-measured at the plateau.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** (1) the mechanism holds as
read: a timing margin in the opaque path, crossed when the core's tile
write-out waits on another reader of the on-die region, more often the
warmer the chip; (2) **on this scene the display's read is that reader,
and with it kept off the core drew 300 of 300 right at 149.50 / 1 at
full speed, warm**: fix (A) has its first proof; the question now is
how to keep the display's reads from colliding without blanking the
screen (the display controller's fetch settings: burst, FIFO, priority
in the display block and `PFCEIL`; or the render timed against the
scan so the two never meet in the same on-die bank: R20's beam race
becomes the stall-free schedule); (3) the ring scene's residual with
the fetch off (GC2AE) means a second reader, its lists, feeds the same
path: the lists on the die beside the fetch off (the all-three way)
is the combination to prove on it; (4) the warm ceiling on this scene
with nothing done is 146.25 / 1; (5) every margin here moves with
temperature, so every proof is a warm proof, and a fast mode keyed to
`TEMP0` stays the fallback design.

**Run GC2BC built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_SCHEDULE=1`, variant
`gc2bc`, 2026-10-27): the stall-free schedule's levers.** GC2BB showed
the display's reads of the on-die region are what make the core's tile
write-out wait, and that the fault needs that wait. Blanking the screen
isn't a fix for a game, so this measures every way of keeping the
display's reads from costing the core its wait, warm, at 149.50 / 1,
each way paired render by render with the unsynchronised baseline, 100
pairs a way (about 30 minutes, unattended):

1. **A ten-minute warm-up** drawing scene 20 (`TEMP0` a minute), so
   every way below is measured at the plateau.
2. **Punch-through, with the object word read back** from the 2700G's
   memory (bit 14 of slot 0's TSP word, set and cleared), 50 pairs: the
   flaw in GC2BB's way was on the device side (the renderer's host test
   proves `set_tsp_or` writes the bit), and the readback says where.
3. **`DMCTRL`, the display's memory control** (`0x2188`): Windows
   Mobile leaves `0x10303010` and Linux writes `0xD0303010`; no field is
   documented anywhere (mbxfb names it, nothing more). Its four byte
   fields (16, 48, 48, 16) read as FIFO thresholds or burst lengths, so
   each is halved and doubled (8 ways) and Linux's tried, with the
   display's underrun flag counted: a setting that starves the display
   is no use whatever it does for the core.
4. **The render begun at a scan line**: the frame started when
   `DVLNUM` reaches 0, 100, ... 500, or 610 (near the blanking). A
   render takes 4.5 ms, about 170 lines. If the on-die memory serves
   the display and the core from separate banks, the rate falls where
   the scan and the tiles being written are far apart, and the beam
   race (R20) becomes the stall-free schedule; a flat rate means the
   arbiter's wait doesn't care where the scan is.
5. **Bands behind the scan** (rp-wmprobe W3's way, the usable form of
   4): the frame in four bands of ten tile rows, each drawn once the
   scan has read past it this frame.
6. **The ring scene** (GC2AE's fault generator, which kept 1.6% with the
   fetch off): as GC2AE, the fetch off, the tile lists on the die, both.
   If both is 0, the lists were its second reader.
7. **The texel phases, warm** (GC2BB measured them on a cooler chip).

Dell's way (a finding): his display plane and the 3D core share the
local memory with his register values and he never ran the core at
divide 1 above 74.75; the old way of ours was one lever a run from
cold. Ours measures the levers on one warm chip, paired, with the
display's own underrun flag as the cost meter. `DMCTRL` is written on
the display block only (no CPLD or power bit); every value is put back
after its pairs and at the end.

**GC2BC on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-27 about 2:55-3:33 AM, image of
e7846170; `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2BC.txt`, card log `gpubench-0129`;
8,826 renders, 0 hung, every reference repeatable, the on-die renderer's
ring picture the reference's, the control at Windows Mobile's clock
after right).** The warm-up took scene 20 from 9% to 28% wrong over ten
minutes (`TEMP0` 881 to 873); the ways ran at 24-49% beside their
baselines, a warmer run than GC2BB's.

- **Punch-through, set the right way: 0 of 50 at 20.2 ms against 14 of
  50 at 4.5 ms beside it.** The object word read back held bit 14. GC2AQ
  stands; GC2BB's void rows were C91's class (its punch way ran after
  the on-die renderer and drew the on-die lists).
- **`DMCTRL`: no field helps.** Each byte halved or doubled, and
  Linux's value, left the rate where its baseline was (24-45 of 100
  against 24-45). Byte 0 doubled and byte 1 doubled starved the display
  (100 underruns of 100; byte 1 doubled also slowed the render 7%): those
  two are the fetch's burst or FIFO depth, and larger is worse for the
  core, not better. The display's fetch settings are not the lever.
- **The scan's position is not the lever either.** The render begun at
  line 0, 100, ... 500 or 610: 35-42 of 100 at every line, the baselines
  30-49. Four bands each drawn behind the scan: 37 against 46. The
  arbiter's wait costs the same wherever the scan is: the on-die memory
  serves the display and the core from one port, not from banks the two
  could be kept apart in. R20's beam race is a tearing design, not a
  stall-free schedule.
- **The ring scene names its second reader: its lists.** As GC2AE 6 of
  100 (3 beside it); the fetch off 1 (6); **the lists on the die 0 (0)**;
  both 0 (5). The rates are low, so 100 pairs can't rank them finely,
  but the lists on the die beside the fetch off is the combination that
  took it to zero, as predicted.
- **The texel phases, warm: 0-5 wrong in 5 of 5, 6-11 right**: GC2AM's
  number exactly (0.009 texel) at the warm plateau; GC2BB's cooler run
  had them right. The coordinate error grows with temperature, and the
  placement rule's margin is the warm number.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the display's reads cost the
core its wait at the memory's one port, and nothing in the display's
settings or timing changes that; the only ways found to a clean render
at 149.50 / 1 at full speed are to keep the display's reads off the
memory the core writes (GC2BB: the fetch off, 0 of 300) and, where the
scene's lists are the reader, the lists on the die (the ring scene, 0 of
100). So fix (A) now has one shape: **the core must render into memory
the display is not reading from.** The display must scan the on-die
region (G19-G21) and the core must write there too, and one 480 x 640
screen is 600 of its 712 KiB: a second full screen doesn't fit. What
fits: a 480 x 320 double buffer (two of 300 KiB) shown through the
video plane's 2x vertical scale (proven in P9), or strips (96 KiB spare)
copied in by the 2D engine between renders. Whether it works at all is
the next measurement, GC2BD: scene 20's faulting rows rendered as a
strip into on-die memory the display never reads, against the same
strip at a displayed address, warm; if the undisplayed strip draws
clean, the design is a double buffer the display isn't scanning, and
the OS's screen size and scaler decide the rest.

**Run GC2BD built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_STRIP=1`, variant
`gc2bd`, 2026-10-27): fix (A)'s one shape measured.** GC2BC left one
way to a clean render at 149.50 / 1 at full speed: the core writing
memory the display is not reading. This measures exactly that, warm,
without changing the display at all:

- **The strip:** scene 20's rows 224-319 (six tile rows: its faulting
  rectangle's top edge on the first tile row's last row, the faults in
  the second) rendered by a 480 x 96 renderer with the scene's draws
  shifted up 224 rows (14 tile rows, so every edge keeps its place in
  its tile; the host model proves the strip is the full scene's rows
  word for word, `gc2bd_strip_is_the_full_scenes_rows`, and the device
  checks the same at Windows Mobile's clock).
- **Two addresses, paired render by render:** the strip where those
  rows sit in the scanned-out target (`0x43800`, shown), and at
  `0x96000`, the on-die memory past the display's 480 x 640 (the
  target's last 64 rows and the 36 KiB after it), which the display
  never reads, inside G19's edge. 300 pairs.
- **The control:** the hidden strip with the display's fetch off,
  paired with the hidden strip, 150 pairs: if hidden is already clean,
  the fetch off adds nothing.
- **The ring scene's lists on the die** at 300 pairs (GC2BC's 0 of 100
  sharpened), with `core_taken` at every switch.
- Fifty full-scene renders after, for the plateau's rate, and the
  control at Windows Mobile's clock.

What each outcome means: **hidden clean, shown wrong** at the same rate
as the full scene: the display's reads collide only with writes to the
addresses it is reading (or their SRAM bank), and the design is a
double buffer the display isn't scanning (a 480 x 320 pair on the die
through the video plane's 2x scale, or strips copied in by the 2D
engine); **hidden as wrong as shown**: the collision is the port's,
address-blind, and only the fetch off or the clock steps remain;
**neither wrong**: the strip doesn't reproduce the fault (the six-row
render's own schedule) and the test must grow. Dell never rendered
where his display wasn't reading; the old way of ours measured with
the target always under the scan. About 25 minutes, unattended.

**GC2BD's first run on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-27 about 3:40-4:08 AM,
image of 318248aa; `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2BD.txt`, card log
`gpubench-0130`; 4,667 renders, 0 hung, every reference repeatable, the
control at Windows Mobile's clock after right).** The warm-up took
scene 20 from 17% to 31% (`TEMP0` 879 to 873); the full scene after the
run: 20 of 50 (40%).

- **The ring scene's reader is its lists: with them on the die, 0 of
  300 wrong at 5,104 us against 17 of 300 at 5,150 us beside it** (by
  chance about 1 in 10^7; and 0.9% faster). GC2BC's 0 of 100 confirmed
  with room. Where a scene's lists are the reader, the lists on the die
  cure it at full speed.
- **The strip's own parts didn't run:** the X51v drew the strip 17
  words off the full scene's rows 224-319 (the model draws it word for
  word), while the strip itself was repeatable at both addresses (0
  words off drawn again, 0 at the hidden address). The run's gate asked
  for both and skipped parts 2 and 3; the pairs need only repeatability
  (the strip is its own reference), so the gate is relaxed and the run
  goes again. The 17 words are a finding of their own: the core's set-up
  of draws clipped at the strip's top edge rounds differently from the
  model's (the shifted draws start above the target), small and
  deterministic; it doesn't touch the question the strip asks.

**GC2BD run 2 on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-27 about 4:00-4:32 AM, image
of 1bb0fa39; `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2BD2.txt`, card log `gpubench-0131`;
5,948 renders, 0 hung, every reference repeatable, the control at
Windows Mobile's clock after right).** Warm (the full scene 23 of 50
after, 46%; `TEMP0` 879 to 871):

| Pairs at 149.50 / 1 warm | way | baseline beside it |
|---|---|---|
| the strip hidden (0x96000) against shown (0x43800), 300 | **0** (688 us) | 3 (689 us) |
| the hidden strip with the fetch off against hidden, 150 | 0 | 0 |
| the ring scene, lists on the die, 300 | **0** (5,104 us) | 20 (5,150 us) |
| **scene 20, lists on the die, 300** | **18** (4,486 us) | **131** (4,500 us) |

- **Scene 20's lists are most of its trigger**: with them on the die,
  6% against 44% beside it, a sevenfold cut, 0.3% faster. GC2BB's void
  row had hidden this. The display's reads are the rest: with the fetch
  off GC2BB had 0 of 300 with the lists in SDRAM.
- **The ring scene's cure holds a second time** (0 of 300 against 20).
- **The strip barely reproduces the fault** (3 of 300 shown, against
  the full scene's 46%): six tile rows at 0.69 ms don't build the
  schedule the full frame does, so hidden 0 against shown 3 is
  suggestive (about 1 in 8 by chance), not proof. The X51v drew the
  clipped strip 17 words off the model's rows again, repeatably.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** two readers put the core's
tile write-out on hold at divide 1, and each measured lever removes
one: the object lists on the die take away the parameter fetch's waits
(all of the ring scene's, seven eighths of scene 20's), and the
display's reads off the target take away the rest (GC2BB). Fix (A) is
therefore two parts, and both fit the die: **the lists on the die, and
the display scanning a buffer the core isn't writing.** The design that
fits 712 KiB: a 480 x 320 picture pair (300 KiB each) shown through the
video plane's 2x vertical scale (proven in P9), the core writing one
while the display reads the other, with 112 KiB on the die for the
lists. GC2BE measures exactly that on scene 20's faulting half: a 480 x
320 target under the video plane's reads against one the display isn't
reading, each with its lists in SDRAM and on the die, warm.

**Run GC2BE built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_VIDEO=1`, variant
`gc2be`, 2026-10-27): fix (A)'s design measured.** GC2BD left fix (A)
as two parts, the lists on the die and the display scanning a buffer
the core isn't writing. The design that fits 712 KiB is a 480 x 320
picture pair on the die shown through the video plane's 2x vertical
scale; this measures it on scene 20's faulting half:

- **The pictures:** A at 0 (300 KiB), B at 0x4B000, the lists on the die
  at 0x96000 (96 KiB; a second renderer; `core_taken` at every switch).
  Scene 20's rows 0-319 rendered by a 480 x 320 renderer (the faulting
  rectangle's rows 239-255 inside); the picture its own reference,
  checked repeatable at both addresses and on the on-die renderer, and
  logged against the full reference's rows.
- **The display:** after the ten-minute warm-up with the planes as they
  are, the graphics plane's reads off and the video plane showing A as
  RGB565 at twice its height (`VideoFormat::Rgb565`, new: the graphics
  format on the video plane, code 7 with the colour conversion off, as
  run D showed exactly; P9's scaler set-up, `scale_to`, bit 27, the
  designed tables). The label band is off the screen meanwhile; the
  screen shows A, the scene, as the design would.
- **The pairs, 300 each at 149.50 / 1 warm:** B (never read) against A
  (read); B with the lists on the die against B; A with the lists on
  the die against A; B with the video plane hidden against B. Then the
  planes put back, fifty full-scene renders, the control.

What each outcome means: **B clean with its lists on the die while A
isn't**: the design is proven on this scene at full speed (B's render
time is the game's); **B as wrong as A**: the display's reads collide at
the port whatever they read, and the design falls back to the display
off during the render or the clock steps; **A and B both clean**: the
half-height render's own schedule doesn't fault and the test must grow
(a 480 x 384 pair would still fit). Dell's driver renders into the
buffer his display scans; ours is the first measurement of the core
drawing beside the display's reads instead of under them. About 20
minutes, unattended.

**GC2BE on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-27 about 5:05-5:32 AM, image of
884558c2; `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2BE.txt`, card log `gpubench-0132`;
6,509 renders, 0 hung, the half picture the full reference's rows word
for word on the device, repeatable at both addresses and on the on-die
renderer, the video plane showing A at 2x (`DSIG` live), the control at
Windows Mobile's clock after right).** Warm (the full scene 32 of 50
after, 64%; `TEMP0` 880 to 871). The half picture renders in 2,245 us:

| Pairs at 149.50 / 1 warm, 300 each | way | baseline beside it |
|---|---|---|
| B (never read by the display) against A (read) | 267 | 267 |
| B with the lists on the die against B | **15** (2,242 us) | 274 (2,245 us) |
| A with the lists on the die against A | **10** (2,242 us) | 273 (2,245 us) |
| B with the video plane hidden (no display reads) against B | 217 | 263 |

- **Which buffer the display reads makes no difference** (267 against
  267): the display's reads cost the core its wait at the port whatever
  address they fetch. The double-buffer-on-die design buys nothing;
  GC2BD's hidden-strip hint (0 against 3) was chance. Dropped.
- **The lists on the die are the lever**: 15 and 10 of 300 against 274
  and 273, an eighteenfold cut, 0.1% faster; the half picture with its
  lists in SDRAM faults in 89% of renders (its 1,200 tiles reach the
  faulting rectangle after four tile rows instead of twenty-four, so
  the parameter fetch's waits land on it early), and the on-die lists
  take that away.
- **The display's reads are the smaller reader here**: with no display
  at all 217 against 263 (a sixth); GC2BB's fetch-off 0 of 300 was on a
  cool chip at a 5% rate, where the display's share was all of it.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** two readers, one big and one
small: the object lists (seven eighths of the full scene's faults
warm, eighteen nineteenths of the half picture's, all of the ring
scene's) and the display's reads (the rest). The lists on the die are
the fix's first part at no cost; what the display's reads leave (5-6%
warm) is the open question, and the combination never measured is the
lists on the die with the display's reads off. GC2BF measures that and
the other levers on top of the on-die lists (the per-tile wait, the
checker order, punch-through, the warm ladder with the lists on the
die), on the full scene, warm.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the texel error splits in two:
samples near a texel edge (clamped textures at whole ratios), cured by
the shift at no cost; the bilinear filter's weights at high
magnification, which no shift reaches and which needs 143.00 / 1. What
decides the design is how much magnification the filter takes at 149.50
before the error shows (a game's textures are magnified 1-4x, rarely
480x): GC2BI measures kind 10's span on quads of side 4 to 256 at
149.50, 146.25 and 143.00 / 1. A limit there becomes a rule ("the 3D
core magnifies at most N times; beyond that the texture is pre-scaled")
or, failing that, the clock steps to 143.00 while such a texture is on
screen.

**Run GC2BI built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_MAGNIFY=1`, variant
`gc2bi`, 2026-10-27): cancelling divide 1's coordinate error at no cost,
and the bilinear filter's magnification limit.** The owner: "I want to
get full divide 1 at 149.50 fast speed fully fixed ... with no
performance hit". GC2BH left a magnified bilinear texture with no cure
at 149.50. Every measurement of the error says it is **one-sided**
(always past the edge, never short: GC2AM, GC2AV), **deterministic**
(the same pixels every render) and **small** (under 0.009 texel warm,
smaller cool). An error like that can be cancelled: the renderer asks
for a coordinate the error's size short, and the core's sample lands
where it belongs. That costs only the numbers written at set-up, no
render time.

- **Part 1, the compensation, at 149.50 / 1 straight after the
  warm-up** (the chip warmest): kind 10 (one 8 x 8 texture's span
  magnified 480 x 640 times) with its coordinates pulled back by 0.004,
  0.008 and 0.012 texel in u alone, v alone and both; pushed forward
  0.008 in both as the sign check; and kind 10 as it is; 100 rounds
  each, checker order, lists on the die. A pull-back that draws it
  right in 100 of 100 is a cancelled offset; none drawing it right is a
  slope error (it grows across the quad), whose cure is the gradient
  compensated instead; the push forward getting worse confirms the
  sign.
- **Part 2, the magnification ladder:** kind 10's span on quads of side
  4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128 and 256, and kind 10 itself, at 149.50, 146.25
  and 143.00 / 1, 100 rounds each: how much magnification the filter
  takes uncompensated.
- The log describes two wrong renders a plan (GC2BH's 64 KiB log
  filled).

The error grows with heat, so a cancelling amount found warm may
over-correct cool. If the amount follows `TEMP0`, the compensation is
keyed to the temperature and the first-docking self-test (R71) measures
each device's curve. Dell has no such correction (his core never ran
here); the textbook treats a sampler's precision as fixed; ours
measures this chip's error and subtracts it. About 25 minutes,
unattended.

is the magnification, not the distance):** the same texture span is
right on a 256 x 256 quad and wrong on the 480 x 640 one, and the fault
starts past the first tile row: the error grows with a pixel's distance
from the primitive's origin, as a per-tile set-up of the texture's plane
equations from the first vertex would (a product of a tiny gradient and
a large distance, rounded), and no fixed pull-back can follow a growing
error. Splitting the primitive keeps every pixel near its own quad's
origin at no cost in tile work (the quads don't overlap: each tile still
draws one object). GC2BJ measures it: kind 10 split into 2, 4, 6 and 8
quads, and squares of 320, 384 and 480 to place the threshold, at 149.50
and 146.25 / 1.

divide 1 shows once a texel is stretched over more than about 256-320
pixels at 149.50 (the per-pixel gradient so small its rounding shows),
480-640 at 146.25. Ordinary textures are drawn at 1-16x; only a tiny
texture used as a smooth fill reaches it. Its no-cost cure: the texture
enlarged once on the CPU (exactly: bilinear blending is linear inside a
texel, so blending the enlarged grid redraws the same surface) so the
core never magnifies past the limit. GC2BK measures it and places the
limit between 256 and 320.

**Run GC2BK built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_UPSAMPLE=1`, variant

blend rounded, the corners the plane's), sampled from the first texel's
centre to the last's: the same surface, magnified 68 x 91 and 32 x 43

is used. Both clean is the cure: the renderer enlarges any texture it
would magnify past the limit, once, at load. Dell's driver has no such
rule (its core never ran here); the textbook raises the filter's
precision, which this core can't; ours keeps the core inside the range
it draws right and spends a few hundred bytes. About 15 minutes,
unattended.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the bilinear error depends on
where samples fall relative to the filter's own quantisation, not on
magnification alone: the 16 x 16 case puts every sample on an exact
1/32-texel step (128 units a pixel), and its faults repeat every 16
pixels, one step low; the squares and kind 10 sweep the fractions
densely and fault one step high. Not yet a model: the filter's weight
precision, its rounding and the direction of the core's error are to be
fitted from these patterns (each wrong pixel's sample fraction computed
on the PC), and a no-cost cure designed from the model (coordinates
that keep samples off the boundaries the error flips, as the half-texel
shift does for texel edges). Proposed: the owner's switch to a stronger
model for that analysis and GC2BL's design (a map of the fault over the
per-pixel texel step and the texture's size).

**Run GC2BL built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_MAP=1`, variant
`gc2bl`, 2026-10-27): the bilinear error mapped.** GC2BI's placements
show the faults are tied to sample coordinates, not pixels: its first
faulty row moved down 2.6 rows per 0.004 texel of v pull-back (rows 10,
13, 15, 18), 640 rows per texel exactly. A shift moves which pixels meet
the failing fractions; a dense sweep meets them anyway. So the cure
must keep samples off those fractions, and this run finds them: twelve
full-screen sweeps of a 16 x 16 ramp (red along u; `v` fixed, so every
row is the same sweep and a wrong pixel's column names its fraction
whatever the core's convention), 50 renders each at 149.50 / 1 warm
with both tile-race cures on, every wrong pixel counted by column (the
complete map), by its sample fraction in 64ths of a texel with the red
error's sign, and by tile row. The sweeps: 1/32 texel a pixel at the
texel-centre phase (GC2BK's wrong case), the same with the phase moved
1/64 texel, with `v` at a quarter, and with `v` between two rows; 7/480
(GC2BK's right case); 1/480 (kind 10's) with `v` fixed and with a `v`
blend; 1/288, 1/272 and 1/256 (the squares); 1/64 and 1/128. The map's
reading (`tools/gc2bl_map.py`): which fractions fail, whether a phase
moves them, whether `v`'s fraction gates them, and whether the period
is the step's binary pattern (a carry chain) or the fraction itself.
About 15 minutes, unattended.

3,726 renders, 0 hung, the texture read back exact, every reference
repeatable).** Warm (the warm-up 41% to 69%); the map read with
`tools/gc2bl_map.py`. Wrong pixels over 50 renders a scene (the
fraction and tile-row counters saturate at 65,535; the column counts
don't):

| Sweep (texel a pixel, step in 1/4096-texel units) | wrong pixels | wrong columns | where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1/32 (128 exactly), texel-centre phase | 1,557,354 | 236 of 480 | every half-texel boundary, runs 1-25 columns |
| 1/32, phase +1/64 texel | 573,050 | 117 | the same boundaries, runs 1-7 |
| 1/32, v at a quarter; v between rows | 1,548,999; 1,554,816 | 236; 236 | as the first: v doesn't matter |
| 1/64 (64) | 54,093 | 65 | the boundaries, runs 1-9 |
| 1/128 (32) | 464,374 | 164 | runs 2-16 |
| 1/256 (16) | 91,721 | 15 | 0.002-0.027 texel past the boundaries |
| 1/288 (14.22) | 95,450 | 9 | 0.002-0.009 past the boundaries |
| 1/272 (15.06) | 800 | 1 | column 408, one tile row only |
| **1/480 (8.53), v fixed; with a v blend** | **0; 0** | 0 | |
| **7/480 (59.73)** | **0** | 0 | |

- **Every wrong pixel is red one step low**: the sample lands short.
  One-sided, the other way from the texel-pick error of GC2AM.
- **The failing samples sit just past a half-texel boundary** (the red
  ramp steps every half texel, so a short sample flips there): at 1/256
  and 1/288 within 0.002-0.027 texel past it, the window widening along
  the sweep; at 1/32 whole quarter-texel runs.
- **Steps that are exact multiples of 1/4,096 texel fail; the odd ones
  don't:** 128, 64, 32 and 16 units a pixel are wrong in hundreds of
  columns; 14.22 in nine, 15.06 in one, 8.53 and 59.73 in none. Kind
  10's own sweep (1 texel over 480 pixels, 8.53 units) is clean here
  with this texture, so kind 10's faults come from its 15-times steeper
  colour ramp over the same coordinates (a 0.002-texel window shows at
  30 steps a texel where it doesn't at 2).
- **The phase moved 1/64 texel halves the count** (the boundary runs
  shorter) but cures nothing.
- **The faults lie in one of the quad's two triangles:** the count by
  tile row falls from the top of the screen to 0 at the bottom in every
  sweep, with `v` fixed, as the top-left triangle's share of each row
  does.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the error is in the core's
accumulation of the texture coordinate across a primitive at divide 1:
short, growing along the sweep, worst when the per-pixel step's low
bits are zero (a carry-chain path failing at the top clock), and in one
triangle's set-up more than the other's. A no-cost cure follows if an
odd step is enough: the renderer nudges any texture span so the
per-pixel step is never an exact multiple of 1/4,096 texel (a
different last bit changes the picture by under a 4,096th of a texel).
GC2BM measures that: the dyadic sweeps with their spans nudged by one
unit, kind 10's ramp drawn with a nudged span, the sweep in the other
triangle (the quad split the other way), and the enlargement rule as
the fallback; then the proof runs with all three cures.

**Run GC2BM built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_NUDGE=1`, variant
`gc2bm`, 2026-10-27): the nudged span.** GC2BL's map points to a no-cost
cure: a per-pixel texture step that is an exact multiple of 1/4,096
texel fails, an odd one doesn't, so the renderer would nudge any span
one unit (under a 4,096th of a texel; no render time). Sixteen
full-screen sweeps at 149.50 / 1 warm, both tile-race cures on, 50
renders each, every wrong pixel counted (column runs, the top fraction
bins with the red error's sign, tile rows: compact, to fit the log):
the 16 x 16 ramp's dyadic sweeps exact (the control) and nudged by +1
and -1 unit (1/32, 1/64, 1/128, 1/256, 1/288); 1/32 exact and nudged
along the other diagonal (the quad's corners rotated one place, so its
two triangles meet the other way; GC2BL's faults sat in one triangle);
the 16 x 16 enlargement nudged in both axes (GC2BK's wrong case); and
**kind 10 itself** (its 8 x 8 plane, its steep ramp) exact, with its u,
v or both spans nudged, and along the other diagonal exact and nudged:
GC2BL's shallow ramp hid kind 10's fault, so the cure is judged on kind
10's own texture. The reading: nudged sweeps clean where exact ones
fail make the nudge the cure (a renderer rule); kind 10 nudged clean is
the case the owner's "fully fixed" needs; a diagonal that changes the
count says the set-up of one triangle is where the error lives.
About 15 minutes, unattended.

**GC2BM on the X51v (R69, 2026-10-27 about 12:01-12:12 PM, image of
ab51dc63; `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2BM.txt`, card log `gpubench-0140`;
3,958 renders, 0 hung, the ramp read back exact).** Warm (the warm-up
