(wrong pixels; "other" errors are in green or blue, the v ramp's
channels):

| Sweep | wrong pixels |
|---|---|
| ramp 1/32 exact (control) | 1,559,013 |
| **ramp 1/32 nudged +1** | **0** |
| ramp 1/32 nudged -1 | 608,838 |
| **ramp 1/64, 1/128, 1/256 nudged +1** | **0, 0, 0** |
| ramp 1/288 nudged +1 (6,828) | 95,450 (unchanged from 6,827) |
| **ramp 1/32 exact, the other diagonal** | **0** |
| ramp 1/32 nudged, the other diagonal | 0 |
| the 16 x 16 enlargement nudged in u and v | 10,176 (47 of 50; 100 of 100 exact in GC2BK) |
| kind 10 exact (control) | 779,404 |
| kind 10, u nudged +1 | 776,470 |
| **kind 10, v nudged +1** | **3,352** |
| kind 10, both nudged +1 | 3,264 |
| kind 10 exact, the other diagonal | 16,376 |
| kind 10 nudged, the other diagonal | 16,070 |

- **The nudge cures the dyadic sweeps, upward only:** the core's
  accumulated coordinate falls short; a step one unit larger outruns
  the shortfall, one unit smaller doesn't.
- **Kind 10's error is on its steep axis:** its v ramp (green and blue,
  30 steps a texel) carries the faults; nudging v cut them 230-fold,
  nudging u did nothing. What is left sits in whole 8-pixel tile
  columns.
- **The diagonal matters:** the same quad split the other way cured the
  exact 1/32 sweep and cut kind 10 48-fold: the error lives in one
  triangle's set-up, as GC2BL's tile rows said.
- 1/288 (not dyadic) didn't move with a one-unit nudge; the 16 x 16
  enlargement improved to tile-column blocks.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the cure's shape is a
coordinate rule at no cost: the per-pixel step biased upward on each
axis by enough to outrun the core's shortfall. One unit was enough for
the shallow ramp, not for kind 10's steep one. GC2BN finds how much:
kind 10 with v nudged by 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 and 32 units (32 is 1/256 texel
across the screen: invisible), both axes, the other diagonal with a
nudge, a nudged start, and 1/288 and the enlargement with bigger nudges.

**Run GC2BN built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_NUDGE_SIZE=1`,
variant `gc2bn`, 2026-10-27): the nudge's size.** GC2BM's runner
(`gc2_sweeps`, now taking a table) over sixteen sweeps at 149.50 / 1
warm, both tile-race cures on, 50 renders each: kind 10 exact (the
control) and with its v span nudged 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 and 32 units (32
units is 1/256 texel across the screen); u and v both +8 and +32; the
other diagonal with v +8 and both +32; the start moved +8 with v +8;
the ramp's 1/288 sweep +8 and +32 and the 16 x 16 enlargement +8 and
+32 (the two a one-unit nudge didn't move). The smallest nudge that
draws kind 10 right in 50 of 50 sets the renderer's bias; none doing
so names the residue in tile columns as a separate per-tile effect.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** four cures together cover every
fault found at 149.50 / 1: (1) the lists on the die and the checker
order (the tile race); (2) the half-texel shift on clamped textures;
(3) every texture span nudged up 8 units (under 1/1,000 texel; no
render time), which clears the dyadic-step faults; (4) a texture the
core would magnify past about 32 times enlarged once at load (exact:
bilinear blending is linear inside a texel) and nudged, about 1% render
time on such a texture only. The combined proof is next (GC2BO), then
the night soak.

**Run GC2BO built, reminder R69 (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_PROVE2=1`, variant

every texture span nudged up 8 units (`Rig::nudge`, applied where every
scene is set, so references and renders alike; `gc2::nudged`

(`Scene::Upsampled10(1)`, the texture read back before use). The flat
corner sweep (23 scenes), the textured sweep (22), the ring scene (300
rounds), the eight 16-bit kinds (100 rounds), then a 20-minute soak over
thirteen scenes. All zeros is the daytime proof of the full cure set;
the night soak (R73) follows it. About 45 minutes, unattended.

**Run GC2BQ built (2026-10-27, `RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_ORDER_HOT=1`): the
tile race's residue at peak heat against the tile order.** GC2BP's flat
sweep showed the residue again while running (6 wrong by round 45 at
the warmest yet). The checker order was chosen in GC2BG over rows,
reversed and columns at cooler temperatures; at peak heat the order that
keeps a tile's write-out furthest from the display's fetch may differ.
What a vendor does: Dell's driver draws tiles in rows and lives with
what the display costs. Ours: measure every order the core offers at the
temperature that leaks, and pick the one with no residue, at no cost
(the order is a register value). As GC2BO's warm-up (10 minutes on
scene 20, the lists in SDRAM), then on the on-die renderer at 149.50 / 1
with the lists on the die: the flat corner sweep (50 rounds) and its
scene 3 alone (300 rounds) in the checker order, scrambled, columns,
columns reversed, and the checker order again last, the control at both
ends so a drift in heat shows as a drift in the control. About 30
minutes, unattended. `gc2::order_ways` builds each plan's order list
(host-tested).

**GC2BQ on the X51v (2026-10-27, `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2BQ.txt`, card
log `gpubench-0144`, sha f9c7881c): no tile order clears scene 3.** TEMP0

| order | the flat sweep, 50 rounds | scene 3 alone, 300 rounds | fastest sweep render |
|---|---|---|---|
| checker | scene 3 7, others 0 | **30** (10%) | 4,446-4,484 us |
| scrambled | scene 3 3, others 0 | **13** (4%) | 4,305-4,385 us |
| columns | scene 3 2, scenes 12 and 13 1 each | **30** (10%) | 4,427-4,509 us |
| columns reversed | 69 over 11 scenes | **3** (1%) | 4,461-4,490 us |
| checker again | scene 3 6, others 0 | not in the run log | 4,448-4,484 us |

- **The order moves the rate but none reaches zero;** the checker control
  is the same at both ends (no drift).
- **The wrong patch is a tile's share of a rectangle,** as corrected in

- **Scrambled is about 2% faster** than checker over the sweep, and leaked
  least among the orders clean elsewhere.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** scene 3's leak is not the tile
order's. The patch's shape (a grey rectangle's whole share of a tile that
also holds the Gouraud background, one green step high) points at the
shading of a flat object in a tile shared with a Gouraud one. GC2BR
tests that: scene 3 with the background flat, with the rectangles drawn
Gouraud with four equal colours (the same picture; a cure at no cost if
it holds), and without the hidden layer, then the whole flat sweep with
the rectangles Gouraud.

**Run GC2BR built (2026-10-27, `RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_SHADE_HOT=1`).** What a
vendor does: Dell's driver draws flat colours with Gouraud off (bit 22
clear) and does not look past it. Ours: draw the same picture through
each shading path at the temperature that leaks and keep the clean one.
As GC2BQ's warm-up, then on the on-die renderer at 149.50 / 1 in the
checker order: scene 3 as is, the background flat
(`gc2::SHADE_FLAT_BG`), the rectangles Gouraud
(`gc2::SHADE_GOURAUD_RECT`), without the hidden layer (300 rounds each),

**GC2BR on the X51v (2026-10-27, `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2BR.txt`, card
log `gpubench-0145`, sha 9a7a27e2): the shading is not the cause.** TEMP0
872 at the start, 865 throughout, 149.50 / 1, checker, lists on the die:

| scene 3 way, 300 rounds | wrong renders | fastest render |
|---|---|---|
| as is | **7** | 4,471 us |
| the background flat | **2** | 4,471 us |
| the rectangles Gouraud, equal colours | **11** | 4,471 us |
| without the hidden layer | **93** (31%) | 4,162 us |
| the flat sweep, rectangles Gouraud, 50 rounds | scene 3 **1**, others 0 of 1,100 | 4,447-4,484 us |
| as is again | **4** | 4,471 us |

- **The green step stays with the background flat:** the same grey `0x8410`
  read `0x8430` over a tile's share of a rectangle (8 x 10 at y 22-31,
  8 x 2 at y 176-177). It is not the Gouraud background leaking into the
  grey, and drawing the grey Gouraud doesn't cure it.
- **Without the hidden layer the rate is 31%** and the faults are the
  tile race's own (four pixels on one row taking the background's
  `0xc618`): taking an object out of every tile, about 7% less time a
  render, makes the race far worse. The hidden layer is part of the cure.
- **The rate swings between runs at the same warmth:** scene 3 as is 2%
  here, 10% in GC2BQ, 14% in GC2BP (TEMP0 863-865 in all three).

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the green step is not a shading
fault. It sits with the tile race: the less work a tile carries, the more
it faults, and the hidden layer's extra work holds it down. The green
step is the race's residue on one geometry, its rate unsteady at peak
heat. The mechanism inside the core is not yet found.

**The green step read across GC2BO-GC2BR's positions (2026-10-27).**
Every wrong pixel of the step in GC2BP, GC2BQ (every order) and GC2BR
lies in scene 3's top-right rectangle, (259, 18)-(443, 178), or its
tiles: the other five rectangles, three of them at the same x range and
two at the same rows, never faulted in 1,800 renders of the scene. Inside
a tile the wrong pixels are always the rectangle's *last* rows of that
tile (rows 6-15, 9-15, 12-15, 14-15 of interior tiles; the whole 3 x 16
share of the right-edge tiles; the whole 8 x 2 share of the bottom-edge
tiles) and never the background's. The rectangle is the last object
shaded in each of its tiles, so the pixels hit are the last pixels the
shading unit writes before the tile is handed to the write-out, and the
patch's size (16-56 pixels) is how long the hold lasts. The reading:
when a finished tile must wait for the previous tile's write-out (held
at the port by the display's read), the last pixels shaded before the
wait get RGB565 green bit 0 set. It fits the levers: more work a tile
(the hidden layer) gives the write-out time and cuts the rate; the
display's fetch off ends it; the lists on the die take away the other
reader. What it doesn't explain is the selector: why that rectangle and
no other, in every order. A selector that deterministic is the handle
on a cure, so GC2BS measures it directly. GC2BO's wider +1 boxes (x 200
to 479, the background too, at 182-242 pixels a render) were the same
step on the warmest run, so the selector is a matter of margin, not a
different fault.

**Run GC2BS built (2026-10-27, `RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_PORT=1`): the selector,
the bit, and five cures at the port.** What a vendor does: Dell's driver
shows the screen from the graphics plane, leaves `DMCTRL` at
`0x10303010`, renders into the buffer the display scans and never ran
the core above 74.75 / 1; GC2BC measured the display's levers under the
lists' stalls, which were seven eighths of the faults then and hid the
display's share. Ours: with the lists on the die and the checker order
the display is the one reader left, so each lever is measured again
where it can show, on the chase's strongest generator (scene 3 without
its hidden layer, 31% in GC2BR), each render paired with the baseline,
and a clean cure is proven at once on the shipped form. GC2BQ's
warm-up, then on the on-die checker renderer at 149.50 / 1:

1. **The forms** (`gc2::CORNER3_SCENES`, 100 rounds each, references
   inside): scene 3 without its layer as is; its top-right rectangle
   alone; alone moved one cell left (240 pixels: every edge keeps its
   place in its tile); alone moved one cell down (208); the six
   rectangles drawn in the opposite order; the grey `0x848484` (RGB565
   green `0x21`, bit 0 already set: a forced bit can't show, an
   arithmetic +1 still can); as is again; scene 3 with its layer.
   Alone still wrong and moved left clean: the position selects (the
   TA's or shading unit's arithmetic at x past 256, or the display's
   fetch pattern); moved clean both ways and alone wrong: the cell;
   alone clean: the other rectangles' lists; the reversed order moving
   the fault to another rectangle: the list order; the grey `0x84`
   clean: a forced bit.
2. **The condition ways** (`GC2BS_WAYS`, 100 pairs each on the as-is
   form, the way's condition set for its render and put back): the
   display's fetch off (the known clean reference); the picture scanned
   out by the video plane at 1:1 (GC2BE's RGB565 path: the other fetch
   engine, its own FIFO and bursts) with the graphics plane's reads off;
   `DMCTRL` bytes 0-3 halved and Linux's `0xD0303010`, the underrun flag
   counted; the target in local SDRAM (the write-out off the die's port,
   the display reading the die as before). Each way's mean render time
   beside the baseline's: a cure that costs time is no cure.
3. **The proof:** every cure way (not the fetch off) at 0 of 100, the
   first three, 300 pairs on the as-is form and 300 on scene 3 with its
   layer.

The put-backs: `DMCTRL` after every way and at the end, the planes after
every video-plane render, the clock at the end. About 30 minutes,
unattended; the forms host-tested (`gc2bs_corner3_forms`).

| way | wrong of 100 | baseline beside it | render time |
|---|---|---|---|
| **the display's fetch off** | **17** | 12 | 4,167 us (4,167) |
| the video plane as scanout | 78 | 49 | 4,166 (4,206) |
| `DMCTRL` byte 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 halved | 56 / 49 / 49 / 46 | 50 / 51 / 39 / 50 | +0-1% |
| `DMCTRL` Linux's | 56 | 46 | |
| the target in SDRAM | 35 | 47 | **5,031 (+19%)** |

- **The display's reads are not this fault's cause:** with the fetch off
  17 of 100 against 12. GC2BB's and GC2BF's 0 of 300 with the fetch off
  were scene 20's, whose reader is the display; scene 3's is something
  else. The video plane's fetch is worse (78); `DMCTRL` does nothing; the
  target in SDRAM is slower and not clean. No port cure, so no proof ran.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the reading before this run
(the hold at the hand-off, the display at the port) was for the wrong
fault: measured on scene 3 without its layer, the fault is the race on
a fixed screen region, tile rows 15-21 columns 47-52, under every order,
object set and display setting, and the display's reads off don't
touch it. What a render still reads from SDRAM with the lists on the
die: the per-tile region array (19,200 B, the core's first fetch for
every tile) and the manager's block. A fixed band of tile rows is what
a fixed address range in that array would give. GC2BT measures it.

**Run GC2BT built (2026-10-27, `RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_REGION=1`): the region
array on the die.** What a vendor does: Dell's driver keeps the region
array and the lists in SDRAM, as the chase did until GC2BB moved the
lists. Ours: the last SDRAM reads of a render taken off, measured
paired, with the array moved within SDRAM and the CPU quiet as controls
so an address effect and the host port are told apart in the same run.
The target at `0x0` (GC2L: the address doesn't matter; the die then has
60 KiB between the picture and the lists). Four renderers share the core
(`core_taken` at every switch): `base` (lists on the die, the array in
SDRAM: the chase's cure set), `base2` (its array 8 KiB further in
SDRAM), `die` (the array on the die at `0x96000`), `die2` (that and the
manager's block on the die at `0xA0000`). After GC2BQ's warm-up: die
against base on scene 3 without its layer (300 pairs), with it (300) and
scene 20 (100); die2, base2 and the CPU quiet against base on scene 3
without its layer (100 each). If die is clean in all three, the proof on
die: the flat and textured corner sweeps, 50 rounds each. Each pair's
render time is logged (an array on the die should be no slower). About
25 minutes, unattended.

**GC2BT on the X51v (2026-10-27, `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2BT.txt`, card
log `gpubench-0147`, sha 14a28160): nothing outside the core touches
scene 3's race.** TEMP0 876 to 867. The die renderer drew the reference
at Windows Mobile's clock (0 words off); the pairs at 149.50 / 1:

| way, against the on-die-lists checker baseline | wrong | baseline beside it | render time |
|---|---|---|---|
| the region array on the die, scene 3 without its layer, 300 | **68** | 43 | 4,167 us (4,167) |
| the region array on the die, scene 3 with its layer, 300 | 3 | 1 | 4,476 (4,476) |
| the region array on the die, scene 20, 100 | 0 | 0 | 4,478 (4,478) |
| the array and the manager's block on the die, 100 | 28 | 22 | 4,167 |
| the array 8 KiB further in SDRAM, 100 | 28 | 25 | 4,167 |
| the CPU quiet, 100 | 23 | 18 | (7,000: the wait) |

- **The region array is not the reader**, nor the manager's block, nor
  the array's address in SDRAM, nor the CPU's reads; the array on the die
  is no faster either (the core hides that fetch).
- **The proof didn't run** (the die side wasn't clean).

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** every reader outside the core
is now measured and out: the display's reads (GC2BS), the lists (on the
die since GC2BB), the region array, the manager's block, the host port.
Scene 3's race is inside the core at 149.50 / 1 warm. What the fault
positions say, read across every run since GC2AJ: each dropped group
(four pixels on a row in the background's colour) sits on the quad's
internal diagonal, the edge its two triangles share: GC2AJ's (436, 242)
against scene 20's diagonal at 439 on that row; GC2BR's (418, 246)
against scene 3's at 421.4; GC2BS's (386, 274) against 389.2 and (403,
258) against 407.6; GC2BQ's (387, 273) against scene 7's at 393.8. And
only in one patch of the screen, x 386-440, y 236-280, under every
order, object set and reader. GC2AJ's other race fault, the whole
left-edge column at x = 18 dropped on rectangles whose left edge sat 2
pixels into its tile (SDRAM lists, a far higher rate), is the same
class: the pixel nearest a triangle's edge. So the fault is the core's
coverage decision on a group straddling a triangle edge, at particular
screen coordinates, when hot, made likelier by an idle pipeline (the
hidden layer cuts it 10-20 times; SDRAM lists multiplied it). GC2BU
measures four forms that change that decision at no cost, and maps the
patch.

**Run GC2BU built (2026-10-27, `RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_EDGE=1`): the diagonal's
forms.** What a vendor does: Dell's untextured path draws with "always,
no depth write" (TSP `0x0E80`), and every scene of the chase took it;
his quads split along one diagonal. Ours: the same picture through the
paths a game and a compositor could take instead, measured on the
strongest generator, each proven on the shipped form in the same run.
GC2BQ's warm-up, then on the on-die checker renderer at 149.50 / 1:

1. **The forms** (`gc2::EDGE_FORMS`, 100 rounds each): scene 3 without
   its layer as is; every object with a depth test, "greater" with
   writes, the background far and the rectangles near (the same picture
   through the depth path: the hidden-surface unit's compare path, never
   run by the chase); the depth written but compared "always" (writes
   alone); the rectangles moved half a pixel right and down (their edges
   off the pixel grid, the diagonal through other sub-pixel places); the
   other diagonal (`Renderer::set_diagonal_flip`: top-left to
   bottom-right); as is again.
2. **The map** (`gc2::EDGE_MAP`, 50 rounds): the top-right rectangle
   alone moved by whole tiles: into the middle-right cell (the race's),
   96 and 48 pixels above it, 48 and 96 below, 120 and 240 left, and
   120 left 48 up. Whether the patch is a place on the screen (the
   coordinates' arithmetic) or the rectangle's own geometry.
3. **The proof:** every cure form at 0 of 100, without and with the
   layer, 300 rounds.

What each outcome means: the depth path clean is a cure a game already
takes and a compositor can take for free; the half-pixel form clean
says the edge on the grid is the trigger (a sub-pixel placement rule);
the flipped diagonal moving the fault says the shared edge is it. About
25 minutes, unattended; the forms and the flip host-tested.

**GC2BU on the X51v (2026-10-27, `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2BU.txt`, card
log `gpubench-0148`, sha 26067e00): no form cures; the flip never ran.**
TEMP0 875 to 867. Scene 3 without its layer, 100 rounds each, lists on
the die, checker:

| form | wrong of 100 | where |
|---|---|---|
| as is | **15**, then 24 at the end | x 289-421, y 246-359 |
| the depth path, "greater" with writes | 25 | x 306-421, y 246-344 |
| depth written, compared "always" | 35 | the same |
| the rectangles half a pixel over | 9 | x 387-431, y 128-274 (moved) |
| the other diagonal | 18 | the same as as is |

The map (the top-right rectangle alone, 50 rounds each): **2 of 50** in
the middle-right cell, at (403, 258); **0** at 48 and 96 pixels above
and below, 120 and 240 left, and 120 left 48 up.

- **The test's flaw (said plainly):** the other-diagonal form never ran.
  It was set through `Renderer::set_diagonal_flip` in `gc2_set_scene`,
  and `gc2_orders` builds its own scenes, so it drew as is (its faults at
  as is's pixels confirm it). GC2BV does the flip in the scene itself
  (the corners rotated), which every runner draws.
- **The depth path makes it worse** (25 and 35 against 15-24): not a cure,
  and a game's depth-tested draws are no shelter.
- **Half a pixel moves it, doesn't end it.**
- **The place is exact:** the rectangle faults only in its own cell; moved
  three tiles in any direction it is clean.
- **The crack, read from the positions:** the middle-right rectangle runs
  (261, 226)-(445, 386); its split joins TR to BL, crossing y 246, 258 and
  274 at x 422.0, 408.2 and 389.8. The dropped groups sit at x 418-421,
  403-406 and 386-389, each just left of that line, and show the
  background (`0xc618`-`0xc637`): pixels neither triangle drew. A crack on
  the shared edge, at one place, hot.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the race's last form on these
scenes is a crack between a quad's two triangles at one screen place,
when the pipeline is idle (the hidden layer cuts it 10-20 times). The
other diagonal is untested. GC2BV tests it properly and two seals. **What
a seal can't reach:** a game's meshes are many triangles sharing edges,
and the driver can't seal each one; a crack in a game is measured only
by a game-shaped scene (R74), and the clock stepped down by `TEMP0` at
peak heat (146.25 / 1, clean in every test) covers games either way.

**Run GC2BV built (2026-10-27, `RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_SEAL=1`): the crack's
seals.** What a vendor does: every GPU relies on its rasteriser's tie
rule to keep shared edges watertight, Dell's driver included; this core
breaks it hot at 149.50 / 1. Ours: seal the edge in software inside the
rectangle, where an overlap can't be seen, measured on the strongest
generator and proven on the shipped scene. GC2BQ's warm-up, then on the
on-die checker renderer at 149.50 / 1 (`gc2::SEAL_FORMS`, 300 rounds
each): scene 3 without its layer as is; the other diagonal (the corners
rotated one place: TL-BR joined instead of TR-BL, the same winding and
picture); each rectangle's diagonal under a band of its own grey
(`gc2::seal_band`: corners 8 pixels from TR and BL along the edges, so
it straddles the diagonal inside the rectangle; its own split crosses
the diagonal once, at the middle); each rectangle drawn again split the
other way; as is again; with the layer as is and banded. The seals sit
in the renderer's slots 8-15 (`gc2::extra_slots`, set by both scene
setters). Each seal clean without the layer is proven on scene 3
without and with it (300 rounds) and over the flat sweep with every
rectangle sealed (50 rounds). About 25 minutes, unattended; host-tested
(`gc2bv_seals`).

**GC2BV on the X51v (2026-10-27, `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2BV.txt`, card
log `gpubench-0149`, sha 0c50e19b): no seal cures; the other diagonal
cracks every render.** TEMP0 873 to 864, the hottest run yet. Scene 3,
300 rounds each, lists on the die, checker:

| form | wrong of 300 | where | fastest render |
|---|---|---|---|
| without its layer, as is | **131**, then 126 at the end | x 289-442, y 176-359 | 4,163 us |
| the other diagonal (corners rotated: TL-BR joined) | **300** | the bottom-right rectangle, x 280-439, y 434-587 | 4,165 |
| a band of the grey over TR-BL | **45** | moved to the top-right rectangle, y 19-177 | 4,299 (+3%) |
| a copy split the other way | **300** | the bottom-right rectangle, as the flip | 4,282 |
| with its layer, as is | 21 | the top-right rectangle, y 48-177 | 4,475 |
| with its layer, banded | 9 | the same | 5,344 |

- **The other diagonal is a deterministic generator:** the bottom-right
  rectangle split TL-BR cracks in every render at peak heat. The crack
  follows the shared edge's geometry; which rectangle and which split is
  fixed, the rate set by heat. The copy drawn over the original carries
  the same split and cracks the same way.
- **The band cuts the crack about threefold and moves it** to the next
  marginal edge: a seal over one edge does not stop the core missing
  another.
- **With the layer the step stays** (21, 9 banded).

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the core's coverage at 149.50 /
1 when hot misses pixels along shared triangle edges, the edge's slope
and screen place deciding which; sealing one edge moves it to another.
No per-quad software cure reaches it, and a game's meshes would meet it
on any marginal edge. This was the owner's last fix of this round (the
owner, 2026-10-27: "i want to try one more fix then give up"), so the
chase is parked (R75) and the interim plan is the temperature guard
(docs/research/divide1-149.md). GC2BW (the game-shaped frames) is built
and not run; the flip form is the strongest generator to start from.

**Run GC2BX built (2026-10-27, `RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_FIXES=1`): the game test
and every driver-level fix in one run** (the owner, 2026-10-27: "build
all of this into another test ... the fix possibilities and the game
test"; his last fix of this round, then the chase is parked). What a
vendor does: Dell's driver sends the core one register table and one
vertex order and never searched either; GC2AR and GC2AY searched the
blocks one render a bit on generators that fault one render in three to
six, so a clean render meant little. Ours: the generator GC2BV found
(scene 3 split TL-BR, 300 of 300 at peak heat) makes one clean render
mean something, so every lever gets one render, and a clean one is
confirmed in pairs with the control and taken straight onto the game
frames. GC2BQ's warm-up, then on the on-die checker renderer at 149.50
/ 1 with the shipped texture rule:

1. **The games** (200 rounds, in turn): GC2BW's watertight warped 5 x 6
   quad mesh flat on black, over a layer, textured (the RGB565 16 x 16
   texture wrapping, about 10 pixels a texel), and depth tested
   ("greater" with writes, the mesh near over a full-screen quad far);
   scene 3 without its layer; the generator. *GC2BW's first form drew
   two overlapping meshes, which the renderer refused (`TooComplex`: its
   128-list cache overflowed and the uncached lists filled the 20 KiB on
   the die); the depth frame is one mesh over a quad.*
2. **The search on the generator, one render a trial:** each bit of the
   render block (`P2D_REGS`, `GC3T_REGS`: 53 words) and the TA block
   (`GC2AY_REGS`: 25 words, each from its value as read), each bit of
   the flat triangles' ISP and TSP words (`Renderer::set_flat_xor`), the
   vertex order rotated one and two places (`Renderer::set_vertex_rotate`:
   the same triangle, the set-up starting from another corner), and the
   hidden layer; a control render every 32 trials (it must stay wrong);
   a clean trial confirmed in 10 pairs with the control.
3. **The proof:** the first three confirmed fixes on every frame, 100
   rounds, with render times beside part 1's.

About 25 minutes, unattended; the levers and the frames host-tested
(`vertex_rotate_and_flat_xor`, `gc2bw_mesh_frames`).

## Test G11: Dell's chip-select-2 timing at divide 1 (built 2026-10-23)

**Built: `rp-glesfeat` test G11** (`RP_GLESFEAT_G11=1
RP_GLESFEAT_G7_FRAME=local/g7-dell-frame.bin cargo xtask codegen-variant
rp-glesfeat g11`, sha 50f30084). G10's lead: Dell's key names the 2700G's
second interface on chip select 2 (`SRAMChipSelect` 2), and his clock
change sets CS2's half of `MSC1` to `0x7DF1` at 74.75 / 1 where the loader
left Windows Mobile's `0x2481`. A new `rp_hal_pxa27x::diag::set_cs2_half`
changes CS2's timing fields only (its type and width held, host-tested on
Dell's two values). Four ways at 74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 / 1: ours with
CS2 as found; ours with Dell's CS2 at divide 1; Dell's frame with it; Dell's
frame with it, his `NINTPW` and clock order. CS2 is put back to Windows
Mobile's with the clock (the crash path too); G11 refuses to run if the
loader's CS2 isn't Windows Mobile's.

**G11 on the X51v (2026-10-23, `local/rp-glesfeat-G11.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0017`, sha 50f30084): CS2's timing is ruled out.** `MSC1` read
back `0x79D47DF1` at 74.75 / 1 (Dell's both halves exactly) and `0x24847DF1`
at 99.67 and 133.25 / 1, Windows Mobile's `0x24842481` at the end; every
reference repeatable (Dell's frame's equal to his picture), the screen on,
every control right: **0 of 20 right in every way** (ours with CS2 Dell's
16,443 / 18,772 / 13,608; Dell's frame 5,075 / 10,190 / 9,336; with his
`NINTPW` and clock order too 5,158 / 10,288 / 9,336).

**Where divide 1 stands (results, not decisions).** Under the loader the
same scene bytes (Dell's own frame), every 2700G register Windows Mobile's
or Dell's (G5-G9), the CPU's bus kept off the chip during the render
(G8, G10) and both chip selects' timing as Dell's (G11) still draw
Gouraud wrong at 74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 / 1, where Dell's driver draws the
same frame right at 74.75 / 1. Not compared, because they can't be read
back: the board CPLD's latches (power and enable lines; the loader writes
its own table at start) and anything HaRET's hand-off changes; compared and
different but not tried: the PXA's `CKEN` (the loader's USB, UART and bit
24 clocks on), its interrupt controller, the backlight PWM's frequency.

## Test G10: the render alone at divide 1 (built 2026-10-23)

**Built: `rp-glesfeat` test G10** (`RP_GLESFEAT_G10=1
RP_GLESFEAT_G7_FRAME=local/g7-dell-frame.bin cargo xtask codegen-variant
rp-glesfeat g10`, sha 7258ce17). G9's lead: every check so far wrote the
scene and read the picture through the CPU's bus with the chip at divide
1, and the wrong words are grey (one byte in all three channels, as a bus
fault that repeats a byte lane would leave). Four ways at 74.75, 99.67 and
133.25 / 1: ours as before; **ours written and read at Windows Mobile's
clock, the clock changed to divide 1 for the render alone** and back before
the read; Dell's frame the same way; ours written and rendered at divide 1
but read at Windows Mobile's clock. Each change followed by the display's
check (ADR-0035 rule 8), counted per setting.

**G10 on the X51v (2026-10-23, `local/rp-glesfeat-G10.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0016`, sha 7258ce17): the CPU's bus is ruled out.** 240 clock
changes around renders, the display sending after every one, every
control right: **0 of 20 right in every way**, and the wrong words as
many with the scene written and the picture read at Windows Mobile's
clock (ours 16,505 / 19,012 / 13,734; Dell's frame 5,201 / 10,337 / 9,441)
as with everything at divide 1 (16,375 / 19,007 / 13,787). **The render
itself writes the wrong words.**

**A lead left by the registry and G9 (results, not decisions):** Dell's
display driver key holds **`SRAMChipSelect` = 2** beside `VLIOChipSelect` =
3: the 2700G has a second interface on the PXA's chip select 2. Dell's
clock change rewrites **both** halves of `MSC1` (`0x0211FFC8`): at 74.75 /
1 CS2's half is **`0x7DF1`** (RDF 15, RDN 13, RRR 7), at Windows Mobile's
clock `0x2481`; under the loader CS2's half stays `0x2481` at every clock
(G9's diff). Not yet tested: Dell's CS2 timing at divide 1.

## Test G9: every register, Windows Mobile against the loader (built 2026-10-23)

**Built: `rp-wmprobe` v5 (exe sha ec545e41) and `rp-glesfeat` test G9**
(`RP_GLESFEAT_G9=1 cargo xtask codegen-variant rp-glesfeat g9`, sha
491f25a5). G7 and G8: Dell's own frame draws right under Windows Mobile
and wrong under the loader, the same bytes, the same compared registers,
polled or not. So both sides now write the same lines: **every word of the
MBX core's block (`0x000`-`0xFFC`), every word of the system block below
the display (`0x000`-`0x1FFC`, the four offsets that froze Windows Mobile
when read there left out), and the PXA27x's clock, memory, PC Card,
interrupt, power, PWM/LCD and GPIO registers** (`diag`'s list). The probe
writes them after Dell's draws in run 1 (`wm div2`) and run 2 (`wm div1`);
G9 after ours at Windows Mobile's clock (`loader div2`) and at 74.75 / 1
(`loader div1`, its wrong words logged). Each line is written before the
next read: a read that freezes the device is the one after the last line.
Diffed on the PC.

**G9 on the X51v (2026-10-23, `local/rp-wmprobe-9.txt` probe v5 sha
a66bf931, `local/rp-glesfeat-G9.txt` and its card log `glesfeat-0015`, image
3e09c785; the system block narrowed to its 56 known registers after v5's
first run froze reading the hole at `0x08C`).** 1,138 words each side:
- Dell's divide 2 against his divide 1 differ in 19: the clock's
  registers, `NINTPW`, `PLLSTAT`, the frame's buffer addresses, two GPIO
  levels (the backlight PWM and a 32 kHz clock: pins always switching),
  `MSC1`. **Every other word of the MBX block's 1,024 is the same at both
  clocks.**
- **Dell's divide 1 (right) against ours (wrong) differ in 44, and every
  2700G word among them is one G5-G7 already copied** (the render table,
  `0x130`, `0x6D0`, the parameter memory's and the TA's words, `NINTPW`)
  or a status/address word. **No hidden chip register is the cause.** The
  rest are the PXA's: the interrupt controller, the backlight PWM, `CKEN`
  (the loader's clocks on for its own USB, UART and bit 24), and `MSC1`'s
  CS2 half (not the 2700G's chip select; CS3's half equal).

## Test G8: the render waited for without touching the chip (built 2026-10-23)

**Built: `rp-glesfeat` test G8** (`RP_GLESFEAT_G8=1
RP_GLESFEAT_G7_FRAME=local/g7-dell-frame.bin cargo xtask codegen-variant
rp-glesfeat g8`). G7's lead: every render of ours, Dell's frame included,
was waited for by reading the status register (`0x12C`) through the bus in
a tight loop for the whole render; Dell's driver leaves the chip alone and
reads it once, on the interrupt. Five ways at 74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 / 1,
every guard as G7's: ours as before (the control); ours and Dell's frame
**with nothing read until 60 ms have passed, then the status once**; ours
and Dell's frame with **the status read once a millisecond**. (Render
times in these ways include the wait.) The memory manager's set-up waits
the same way in each. `emu-glesfeat` g8: every way draws our scene's one
picture.

**G8 on the X51v (2026-10-23, `local/rp-glesfeat-G8.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0014`, sha 9b497017): polling is ruled out.** Every reference
repeatable (Dell's frame's equal to his own picture), the screen on at all
15 settings, every control right; **0 of 20 right in every way at every
clock**, the chip left alone or read once a millisecond, ours (16,469 /
19,126 / 13,506 wrong words with the chip left alone, against 16,375 /
19,067 / 13,539 polled tightly) and Dell's frame alike (5,222 / 10,305 /
9,385). What the loader leaves different from Windows Mobile lies outside
the registers compared so far (the MBX block's `0x600`-`0x6FC` and
`0x800`-`0x87C`, the system block's named registers) and the frame's
memory: next proposed, every word of both register blocks (and the PXA's
clock and memory registers) read under Windows Mobile after Dell's draws
and under the loader after ours, and diffed.

## Test G7: Dell's own divide-1 frame, replayed word for word (built 2026-10-23)

**The capture (`rp-wmprobe` v4, `local/rp-wmprobe-7.txt`, exe sha
c6d14b4c).** Windows Mobile booted with Dell's `CoreClock` (the probe's run
2: the setting deleted first, Dell's 20 draws at 74.75 / 1 right and equal
to run 1's divide-2 reference, a fifth time), then the 2700G's whole 16 MiB
local memory copied to the card read-only in 12.8 s
(`rp-wmprobe-div1-vram.bin`, kept in `local/`, never committed: CLEANROOM).
The file was taken off the card through the loader's USB drive (the
loader's `get` stalls on 16 MiB).

**The frame (`cargo xtask g7-frame`, `local/g7-dell-frame.bin`, 180,968
bytes).** From the capture and run 2's register state: his region array at
`0x1D6000` (`0x608`), **2,400 entries (60 x 40 tiles, 8 bytes each, the
last flagged): each tile its own object list**, **2 objects** (his clear
and the quad) from his parameter memory at `0x291000` (`0x60C`, 8 MiB to
`0xA91000`, page table `0x1C0000`), lists and objects on 39 pages from
`0x564000`; 44 pages in two segments; his RGB565 target at `0xB27000`,
hashed (`0x8BF77FEF`, 153,600 words). His registers: the render block as in
G5's list, the TA's, `0x130` `0x87F5` (`frame::NEVER` aside: the reset,
status, starts).

**The test (`rp-glesfeat` G7; device build `RP_GLESFEAT_G7=1
RP_GLESFEAT_G7_FRAME=local/g7-dell-frame.bin cargo xtask codegen-variant
rp-glesfeat g7`).** Three ways at 74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 / 1 with every
guard of G5-G6: ours; **Dell's frame replayed** (his pages written into
local memory, his parameter memory set up through our sequence with his
four words, his registers, then the 3D stage started: no object, list or
region of ours); the same with his `NINTPW` rule and clock order. Each
replay's reference at Windows Mobile's clock is held to **his own picture**
(the hash): equal means the replay is his frame exactly. The image refuses
a frame over its own label, reference or fence. Host tests: the frame file
(round trip, every broken file refused), the extraction (every page the
render reads and nothing else, a region array with no end refused), the
layout from his registers (an off-by-one page found and fixed), the local
frame checked when present. `emu-glesfeat` g7 replays a frame of our own
scene and must draw way 1's picture exactly.

**What it decides (results, not decisions):** his frame right under our
loader at divide 1 puts the fault in our scene's bytes (then diffed with
his word by word); his frame wrong under our loader puts it outside the
2700G's registers and memory.

**G7 on the X51v (2026-10-23, `local/rp-glesfeat-G7.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0013`, sha e74c88be): Windows Mobile's clock set first, every
reference repeatable, the screen on at all 9 settings, every control right,
every layout read back as written.**

- **The replay is Dell's frame exactly:** both replays' references at
  Windows Mobile's clock hash `0x8BF77FEF`, **his own divide-1 picture's**
  (153,600 words each).
- **And it is wrong at divide 1 under our loader:** 0 of 20 right at
  74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 / 1 (5,084 / 10,270 / 9,370 wrong words; with
  his `NINTPW` and clock order 5,124 / 10,479 / 9,417), where his driver
  drew that very frame right at 74.75 / 1 five times. Ours, alongside: 0 of
  20 (16,522 / 18,983 / 13,623).
- **So the fault isn't in anything in the 2700G's memory or registers**:
  the same bytes and the same register state draw right under Windows
  Mobile and wrong under our loader. What differs is how the render is
  run. One difference stands out, read from both drivers: **ours polls the
  core's status register (`0x12C`) in a tight loop through the CPU's bus
  for the whole render** (`run`); Dell's driver never touches the chip
  while it renders (GLES waits in `Sleep` loops on counters in RAM; his
  interrupt thread reads `0x12C` once, when the interrupt fires:
  `WaitForSingleObject`, `InterruptInitialize`, the ISR at `0x021192B4`).
  (Results, not decisions: G8 proposed, the render waited for without
  touching the chip.)
- Render times: the frame's renders at 74.75 / 1 take 19.9 ms against
  19.6 at Windows Mobile's 99.67 / 2: with the memory at 74.75 MHz the
  faster cores gain nothing on this frame; ours 12.56 against 12.95.

## Test G6: Dell's places in local memory, his parameter memory and his flow (built 2026-10-23)

**Built, waiting for the device: `rp-glesfeat` test G6** (`RP_GLESFEAT_G6`;
device build `RP_GLESFEAT_G6=1 cargo xtask codegen-variant rp-glesfeat
g6`). G5 ruled out the chip's registers and the clock path; what's left
is what is drawn and where. Dell's places, read by `rp-wmprobe` v3 at
74.75 / 1: **his parameter memory is 8 MiB** (`0x83C` `0x22B000` the base,
`0x828` `0x22C` the first page, `0x82C` `0xA2A` the last: 2,048 pages;
`0x824` `0x1F8000` the page table) where ours is 1 MiB at `0x600000`; his
RGB565 target at `0xE9D000`, his region array at `0xF35000` (`0x608` and
the TA's `0x844`), his TA tail at `0xF40000` (`0x840`). Four ways, each its
own reference at Windows Mobile's clock, 20 renders at 74.75, 99.67 and
133.25 / 1 (`Speed::ladder`), the guards as G5's: (1) ours; (2) Dell's
parameter memory (place, size, table), the rest ours (the target and TA
buffers moved out of its way); (3) all of Dell's places and his RGB565
target, with G5's whole state; (4) the same through the TA as Dell's
driver sends it (his colour clear, then the quad as one strip). Each
way's addresses read back from the core after its render. Host tests:
no buffer of a layout overlaps another or the image's own (the label and
reference move out of Dell's 8 MiB in G6), Dell's places held to the
probe's reading (our parameter-memory set-up writes his four words
exactly).

**The first run (2026-10-23, `local/rp-glesfeat-G6.txt`, sha 65827ef1) tested nothing: the test's fault.** Windows Mobile had booted with Dell's `CoreClock` still set, so the chip was at 74.75 / 1 when the image started, and G6 drew its references there, assuming Windows Mobile's clock; none repeated (1,829, 1,874, 524 and 901 words between two renders; 10.1 ms against 12.95), so no way ran at divide 1. Fixed for every G test: Windows Mobile's clock is set at the start, not assumed (sha 2a105a45).

**G6 again, fixed (2026-10-23, `local/rp-glesfeat-G6b.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0012`, sha 2a105a45): Windows Mobile's clock set first, every
reference repeatable, the screen on at all 12 settings, every control
right, every layout read back from the core as written. 0 of 20 right in
every way at every clock:**

| Way | 74.75 / 1 | 99.67 / 1 | 133.25 / 1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1: ours, our places | 0 (16,548 wrong words) | 0 (18,871) | 0 (13,579) |
| 2: Dell's 8 MiB parameter memory | 0 (16,396) | 0 (19,159) | 0 (13,935) |
| 3: Dell's places, his RGB565 target, his state | 0 (2,050 words: two pixels each) | 0 (5,243) | 0 (5,162) |
| 4: the same through the TA, as Dell's driver sends it | 0 (4,618) | 0 (8,448) | 0 (7,955) |

- **Placement, the parameter memory's size and set-up, and the TA flow
  are ruled out**: way 4 is Dell's places, his target format, his whole
  register state and his sequence through the TA, and is wrong where his
  driver is right. (RGB565 words hold two pixels: their counts aren't
  comparable with ARGB8888's, and the "one step" count means nothing
  there.)
- **The 8 MiB parameter memory works** (way 2's references right and
  repeatable with pages to `0xA2A`): `PM_REACH`'s 8 MiB limit is wrong for
  the 3D stage's direct path (the TA's case, version 14's, not retested).
- **The CPU's bus timing to the 2700G matches Dell's**: `rp-wmprobe` v3
  read `MSC1` `0x24842481` at Windows Mobile's clock (CS3 RDF 8, RDN 4, RRR
  2: our `CS3_WM`) and `0x79D47DF1` at Dell's 74.75 / 1 (CS3 RDF 13, RDN 9,
  RRR 7: our `CS3_LONG`, which G sets below 99 MHz).
- **What's left (results, not decisions):** something none of our copies
  carries: the bytes of Dell's scene itself (his objects, lists and region
  array as his driver lays them in memory), or something outside the
  2700G's registers and memory. Next proposed: capture Dell's frame from
  local memory after his divide-1 draws under Windows Mobile (read-only,
  as `rp-wm3d` did) and replay it word for word under the loader.

**A contradiction to verify (the work-the-audit rule):** `rp_drv_mbx3d`'s
`PM_REACH` says the parameter memory must lie below 8 MiB (version 14:
with it at 12 MiB the TA wrote its lists 4 MiB lower, "11-bit page
numbers, likely"). Dell's own set-up runs his parameter memory to
`0xA2B000` (10.2 MiB, last page `0xA2A`, 12 bits) and draws right. G6's
ways 2-4 use Dell's; their read-back and pictures settle which holds.

## Dell's chip state after his divide-1 draws (rp-wmprobe v3, X51v, 2026-10-23)

**`rp-wmprobe` v3 (`local/rp-wmprobe-6.txt`, exe sha b2d971f7), both runs:
after module 1's 20 draws, with the GLES context still open, every named
system-block register below the display block, Dell's unnamed `0xC0`-`0xC8`,
`0x300`, `0x304`, and the MBX block's `0x080`, `0x12C`-`0x134`,
`0x600`-`0x6FC` and `0x800`-`0x87C` read under Windows Mobile. No read
froze the device. Dell drew right at 74.75 / 1 a fourth time (20 of 20,
identical to run 1's divide-2 reference).**

- **Between Dell's divide 2 (run 1, 99.67 MHz) and divide 1 (run 2, 74.75
  MHz) only these differ:** the clock's own (`COREPLL` `0xBB1`/`0xBC1`,
  `MBXCLK` `0x6`/`0x2`, `M24CLK` 2/0, `LMTIM`, `LMREFRESH`), **`NINTPW`
  `0x1C`/`0x15`** (28/21: the static read's 290 ns, confirmed), **`PLLSTAT`
  `0x5`/`0x7`** (bit 1 set at divide 1), and the MBX block's buffer
  addresses for that frame (`0x608`, `0x60C`, `0x65C`, `0x824`-`0x82C`,
  `0x83C`-`0x844`). Every other word of 153 is the same at both clocks.
- **The MBX block as Dell leaves it at divide 1** (non-zero words):
  `0x130` `0x87F5` (the interrupt enables), `0x610` `0x30001`, `0x614`/`0x618`
  `0x1E3CE500`, `0x61C` `0x20007`, `0x648` 1, `0x650` 1 (RGB565), `0x654`
  `0x01DF0000`, `0x658` `0x027F0000`, `0x660` `0xF0`, **`0x6D0` 1 (read for
  the first time: never in any table G4 tried whole)**, `0x6D8`
  `0x09000000`, `0x6DC` `0x3F800001`, `0x848` `0x00FF0004`, `0x84C`
  `0x01E00000`, `0x850` `0x02800000`, `0x854` `0x3727C5A0`, `0x858`
  `0x3727C5AC`, `0x85C` `0x01D49CE1`, `0x860` `0x00200030`, and the
  addresses above; `0x080` (the reset) 0; every other word of
  `0x600`-`0x6FC` and `0x800`-`0x87C` 0.
- **The system block at divide 1** (non-zero): `PFCEIL` `0x03FDFFE0`,
  `NINTPW` `0x15`, `MINTENABLE` `0x800F`, `SINTSTAT` `0x10`, `SYSCLKSRC` 2,
  `PIXCLKSRC` 1, `COREPLL` `0xBC1`, `DISPPLL` `0xEC5`, `PLLSTAT` 7, `PIXCLK`
  1, `MEMCLK` 1, `MBXCLK` 2, `SDCLK` 1, `PIXCLKDIV` 1, `LCD_CONFIG`
  `0x2EFA0000`, `ODFBSTAT` 4, `GPIOCGF` 1, `PWMCFG` 3, `PWM0PER`/`PWM1PER`
  4, `ID` `0x01727189`, `LMCFG` `0x27`, `LMTYPE` `0xAC8`, `LMTIM` `0x62272`,
  `LMREFRESH` `0x490`, `LMPROTMAX` `0x03FDFFFC`, `0x300` 3, `0x304` 3;
  `0xC0`-`0xC8` 0.
- **For G5 (results, not decisions):** the copy under the loader takes
  every word above except the frame's buffer addresses (ours point at our
  own buffers) and the read-only status words (`PLLSTAT`, `SINTSTAT`,
  `ODFBSTAT`, `ID`, `0x12C`), each logged against ours before it's
  written; then halved. Candidates not yet tried at divide 1: `NINTPW` 21,
  the enables `0x130` `0x87F5`, `0x6D0` 1 with Dell's whole table, the TA
  registers' values, `PLLSTAT` bit 1's source.
- **Built 2026-10-23: `rp-glesfeat` test G5** (`RP_GLESFEAT_G5`; device
  build `RP_GLESFEAT_G5=1 cargo xtask codegen-variant rp-glesfeat g5`).
  Eighteen items (`DELL_ITEMS`): Dell's 16 MBX words above but the
  target's four (`0x130`, `0x610`-`0x61C`, `0x648`, **`0x6D0`**, `0x6D8`,
  `0x6DC`, `0x848`-`0x860`), **`NINTPW` by Dell's rule** after each clock
  change, and **Dell's clock order** (both MBX clocks off across the divider
  write). G4 could only replace words our start table already writes, so
  `0x6D0`, `0x130` and the TA's words never reached the core there; G5
  writes them before every render and reads them back. Ways: ours (the
  control), then ours with all eighteen, each at 74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 /
  1 (`Speed::ladder`, CL2), 20 renders, a control at Windows Mobile's
  clock, `display_check` after every clock change; between them the chip
  compared word by word with Dell's at 74.75 / 1 after ours drew. Where ours
  is wrong and all eighteen right, the items are halved (delta debugging,
  at most 30 checks of one setting each, each its own reference) to the
  fewest still right, and those verified at all three settings. **The
  inherited way:** if the image starts at the cores' divide 1 with a clock
  running (Windows Mobile booted with Dell's `CoreClock`, the loader started
  after), our quad is drawn first on the chip exactly as Dell's driver left
  it (only our memory manager set up), the state compared with Dell's as
  found, and the picture held to ours at Windows Mobile's clock: right
  there means the fix is in what our set-up does to the chip. Host tests 7
  (every value held to the probe's log when present, `NINTPW` to both
  clocks read, the halving: one item anywhere, pairs apart, never the empty
  mask, the limit); 4 mutants. `emu-glesfeat` G5 (the fork draws divide 1
  right, so an emulator-only culprit, item `0x6D0`, makes the halving run:
  found in 6 checks, verified).

## Dell's register writes on the 3D path, by moment (static, 2026-10-23)

Read for the divide-1 question after G4 (the owner's method: the static
list first, then one Windows Mobile run that reads every register on it
after Dell's draws at divide 1, then G5 copies that state under our
loader). Sources: `ddi_2700g.dll` (image base `0x020FD000`) and
`libGLES_CL.dll` (`0x0225C000`) from the A08 ROM (A), disassembled; every
call of Dell's register helpers resolved to its offset and value where
constant (`local/re`, scripts in the session's scratchpad: findings only).

**The helpers.** The display driver writes a register through
`0x0211ED7C(base, offset, value)` (a `str value, [base, offset]` at
`0x0211DA28`), reads through `0x0211ED74(base, offset)`, and writes
`(offset, value)` lists through `0x0211ED8C(base, count, list)`; 112 call
sites in all. Two bases: the **system block** (settings `+0`, the virtual
address of `0x0FFE0000`) and the **MBX core's block** (device `+0x2C`,
`0x0FFE4000`), so an offset below is the system block's unless marked
MBX. GLES writes through `0x02270708` and the list writer `0x0226F4A0`
(the 3D-path section above).

**Dell's settings structure** (`0x10C` bytes, allocated and filled by
`0x02122064` from `HKLM\Drivers\Display\PowerVR`, kept at the driver's
context `+0xA4`): `+0` the system block's address, `+0x10` `Prefetch`,
`+0x30` `LocalMemoryTimeout` (default 10,000; a value under 10 becomes
10), `+0x38` `UseSelfRefresh` (default 1), `+0x40` `LocalMemoryPriority`
(default 251; over 255 becomes 251), `+0x44` `CoreClock` (default
133,000,000; clamped to the memory's maximum at `+0x4C`), `+0x48` the
system clock in Hz as set, `+0x50`/`+0x54` the MBX and M24 dividers
chosen, `+0x74` the memory-timing table (`0x0213F914`), `+0x108` the
saved-register table for resume.

| Finding | Confidence | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **`LocalMemoryTimeout` and `LocalMemoryPriority` are not registers.** Settings `+0x30` is read in one place: the sleep, in ms, of a background thread (`0x0211D964`: `WaitForSingleObject(event +0x3C, 1000)`, `Sleep(+0x30)`, then, when the memory's power mode is 2 and nothing is pending, the local memory is put into self-refresh by `0x02120244`); the escape handler (`0x02101CB0`, request 12) reads and writes `+0x30` and `+0x38` at run time. Settings `+0x40` is read in one place: `CeSetThreadPriority(thread, +0x40)` for that thread (`0x0211DE0C`). No other load of either field exists in the driver (every load at offsets `0x30`/`0x40` traced to its base) | confirmed (code) | `0x021026A0`-`0x021026DC`, `0x0211D98C`, `0x0211DE58`, `0x02120B50` (the settings pointer kept at the thread's structure `+0x178`) |
| **Init** (`0x0211D440` and the settings loader): `MINTENABLE 0x18` = `0xF`, then `0x800F`; `0x304` = `Prefetch` (settings `+0x10`); then the clock init `0x02120730`: `SDCLK 0x58` = 1, `MEMCLK 0x4C` = 1, the memory timing (`0x0211FB98`, below), `LMCFG 0x1004` = `0x27`, the memory power sequence `0x0211FD74`, `SYSCFG 0x0` = `0xFFFFFF00` when `SDRAMMemType` (`+0x8`) is set, bit 0 when `SPAccessViaVLIO`, the clock change below, then `MBXCLK 0x54` = 0 and `((divider - 1) << 2) \| 1` | confirmed (code) | `0x02120DA0`, `0x02120DE0`, `0x021227DC`, `0x02120730`-`0x021207FC` |
| **The clock change `0x02120370(hz)`**, in order: the display planes' enables cleared (`0x2060`, `0x2064`, `0x2100` bit 31, `0x0211FE70`); `ODFBPWR 0x64` = 2 (sleep); the memory power sequence if its mode is 2; `SYSCLKSRC 0x2C` = 0 (13 MHz), 250 µs, the PXA's static-memory timing for 13 MHz (`0x0211FFC8`: `MSC` through `0x48000000`, `KernelIoControl`); the PLL (`0x0211F980`: `COREPLL 0x38` = the found value **with bit 0 set**, `PLLSTAT 0x40` bit 2 polled up to 10 x 5 ms; no lock: `COREPLL` = 0); `SYSCLKSRC` = 2 (the PLL), 250 µs, the PXA timing for the new clock; **`MBXCLK 0x54` = 0 (both clocks off), then `(divider - 1) << 2 \| the enables as read`**; `M24CLK 0x50` the same way; the memory timing (`0x0211FB98`); `ODFBPWR` = (`+0x64` ? 1 : 0) \| (clock < 66 MHz ? 4 : 0); the planes' enables put back. The divider: 1 up to 75 MHz, else the smallest of 2-4 with clock / divider <= 75 MHz | confirmed (code) | `0x02120370`-`0x02120714` |
| **The memory timing `0x0211FB98`**, at init and every clock change: the clock's period p in 10 ps units (`10^8 / kHz`); `LMTIM 0x1018` from the table's ns fields as `ceil(ns / p)`; `LMREFRESH 0x101C`; `LMTYPE 0x1014` with CL by clock (bits 10-11: 1, 2 above table `+4`, 3 above `+8`); **`NINTPW 0x14` = `29000 / p`: the cycles in 290 ns** (28 at 99.67 MHz, as Windows Mobile shows; **21 at 74.75 MHz, 38 at 133.25 MHz**; ours stays at 28 at every clock: the one system-block difference G4's comparison saw and set aside) | confirmed (code); the 28 measured (P) | `0x0211FBAC`-`0x0211FD14`; `rp-wmprobe` run 5 |
| **Resets**: `0x02118738` (the 3D core, before first use): MBX `0x80` = `0x111` (when the device's `+0x24` bit 0) or 0, 500 µs, the interrupts masked, `0x80` ^= `0x111`, 500 µs, cleared, enables `0x87F5`. `0x02120CD8(kind)`: **system block** `0xC0` (kinds 1-2), `0xC8` (3) or `0xC4` (6) = 1, 1 ms, = 0, 1 ms, from the render-context paths `0x02119524`/`0x02119F44` (corrects the earlier guess that `0xC0`-`0xC8` are the MBX block's) | confirmed (code) | `0x02118738`-`0x021187C8`, `0x02120CD8`-`0x02120D8C` |
| **The context set-up `0x0211C744`** (from the render start): MBX `0x80C` = `(y << 17) \| x` of the tile counts, `0x808` = 1, `0x12C` bit 4 polled (TA done), the interrupt cleared, **`0x848` = `0x7FFF`** (GLES writes `0x00FF0004`), **`0x85C` \|= 8** (the value kept at the device `+0x148`), then the context's own `(offset, value)` list (`+0x34`, count `+0x30`), `0x608` replaced by the region base | confirmed (code) | `0x0211C7FC`-`0x0211CA5C` |
| **Before each render `0x0211881C`**: `0x6EC` = the render target's buffer address for this frame (also kept at the render state `+0x7C`); for a new context the set-up above, else `0x85C` = the kept value; the context's list. `0x02118F68`: a 2-pair list (`+0x28`) and **the 15-pair render table** (`+0x10C`): `0x608` = region base, `0x60C` = parameter base (`& 0x1FFF000`), `0x610` 0, `0x644` 0, `0x650` 6, `0x654`/`0x658` `(w - 1) << 16`/`(h - 1) << 16`, `0x660` stride, `0x6D8` `0x01000000`, `0x61C` `0x20004`, `0x614` 0, `0x618` 0, `0x648` 0, `0x6DC` 0, `0x680` 1: **our `start_table`'s defaults word for word**; the values the probe reads back (`0x30001`, `0x1E3CE500`, `0x20007`, `0x648` 1, `0x09000000`, `0x3F800001`) are GLES's 19-pair table's (above), tested by G4. `0x02119088`: `0x85C` = the kept value, `0x804` = 1 | confirmed (code) | `0x0211B5B4`-`0x0211B778` (the table built), `0x02118FA4`-`0x0211902C`, `0x021189B4`-`0x021189D4` |
| **Interrupts**: enable `0x130` set/cleared (`0x0211F408`/`0x0211F464`), status cleared through `0x134` (`0x0211F4C0`), all MBX; at resume `0x4130` from the system block (the same register) | confirmed (code) | |
| **The 2D job path**: `(offset, value)` lists from a job structure (`+0x14`, count `+0x18`), then MBX `0x30C` = 1 (`0x0211D354`, `0x0211D250`); `0x60` from `0x0211D30C` | likely (code) | |
| **GLES, besides the TA and render tables above**: three short TA lists at each scene (`0x0226FE94`: `0x864` = `+0x38 & 0x1FFF000`, `0x820` = `+0x34 & 0x1FFFE00`, `0x818` = 1; `0x0226FF64`: `0x864`, `0x820`, `0x810`, `0x814` = 1; `0x02270050`: `0x810`, `0x81C` = 1), `0x85C` bit 7 set or cleared around a wait (`0x0226FE14`-`0x0226FE78`), `0x834` = 1 (`0x02270464`) | confirmed (code) | |

**What this leaves for divide 1.** Every system-block and
memory-controller register Dell writes is now known and, `NINTPW` aside,
matches ours (G4's comparison). The 3D core's own registers outside the
17 the probe read (`0x600`-`0x6FC` as a whole, the TA's `0x800`-`0x87C`,
the reset `0x80`, the enables `0x130`) were never read under Dell at
divide 1: that is step 2's probe. The sequence differences (the clocks
off while the divider is written, the display planes off during the
change, Dell's TA kick before every render) are G5's ways after the
state copy.

## Test G4: Dell's render table doesn't fix divide 1 (2026-10-23)

**`rp-glesfeat` test G4 on the X51v (`local/rp-glesfeat-G4.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0009`, sha fa3d719c): ten ways at 74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 MHz /
1 (CL2), 20 renders each; the screen on at all 30 settings, every control
right; Dell's words read back from the core after each way's first render,
exactly as written.**

- **Dell's render table doesn't fix it:** his seven differing words
  together (`0x610`, `0x614`/`0x618`, `0x61C`, `0x648`, `0x6D8`, `0x6DC`),
  with our 32-bit target and with his RGB565 one (his set-up exactly), and
  each word alone: **0 of 20 right at every clock**. What makes Dell's
  driver right at divide 1 lies outside the render table.
- **`0x61C` `0x20007` changes the Gouraud picture itself** (alone or in the
  table: hash `0xD0E19F8C` against ours `0x4A8221C0`): GC2F's rounding mode,
  seen again. RGB565 gives its own picture (`0xFD999FD5`).
- **A false pass, caught after the run:** G2's colourless clear showed "20
  of 20 right" at all three clocks, but its reference was the all-white
  picture (hash `0xF748ADC5`, the same as its background drawn alone): the
  quad never drew, and white matched white. Drawing the colourless clear
  alone first (G3 and G4 do, G2 didn't) evidently leaves the next renders
  empty (white is what the core writes where nothing is drawn). New chip
  fact; the test's hole closed: a reference that equals its background
  alone, or whose quad corners read alike, is refused.

## Test G3: no background fixes divide 1; Dell's own sequence wrong under our set-up (2026-10-23)

**`rp-glesfeat` test G3 on the X51v (`local/rp-glesfeat-G3.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0007`, sha 31febc18; it finished before the owner's reset):
eight backgrounds under GC2's ARGB8888 Gouraud quad, each at 74.75, 99.67
and 133.25 MHz / 1, 20 renders each, every background also drawn alone.
The screen went dark after way 1's first setting (quality audit C69): from
there on every setting ran with the display not fetching.**

| Way (background) | 74.75 / 1 | 99.67 / 1 | 133.25 / 1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1: our giant flat triangle | 0 right (16,592 wrong words) | 0 (19,052) | 0, none finished |
| 2: Dell's clear, a flat quad, by hand | 0 (21,355) | 0 (23,579) | 0, none finished |
| 3: Dell's clear and a four-corner quad | 0 (20,387) | 0 (21,191) | 0, none finished |
| 4: flat screen-sized triangles | 0 (7,428) | 0 (8,020) | 0, none finished |
| 5: the giant triangle at z 1.0 | 0 (19,805) | 0 (24,061) | 0, none finished |
| 6: a Gouraud giant triangle | 0 (19,755) | 0 (24,119) | 0, none finished |
| 7: no background | 0 (21,254) | 0 (24,502) | 0, none finished |
| 8: Dell's clear and strip through the TA | 0 (21,070) | 0 (19,520) | 0, none finished |

- **No background fixes divide 1, and none is needed to break it:** with no
  background at all the quad is as wrong as under ours. Every wrong word is
  again grey (channels equal) and inside the picture. G2's one fix, the
  GLES clear's **colourless** object (TSP `0x22206F80`, layout 0), was not
  among G3's ways: it remains the only form seen right at divide 1.
- **Dell's exact sequence (way 8, as `glClear` and a strip of four send it)
  is wrong under our register set-up**, where Dell's driver draws it right
  (`rp-wmprobe`, three runs). So what still differs is the set-up, not the
  objects. Dell's render table at 74.75 / 1 (`rp-wmprobe` run 2) against
  ours (`start_table`): `0x610` `0x30001` (ours 0), `0x614`/`0x618`
  `0x1E3CE500` (0), `0x61C` `0x20007` (`0x20004`), `0x648` 1 (0), `0x650` 1,
  RGB565 (6, ARGB8888), `0x6D8` `0x09000000` (`0x01000000`), `0x6DC`
  `0x3F800001` (0). GC2F tried Dell's words with our renderer and found no
  change; it didn't take `0x610`, `0x614`/`0x618`, `0x6DC` or an RGB565
  target with them all at once.
- **At 133.25 MHz / 1 no render finished: the test's fault.** G3 set the
  local memory by Dell's rule, CL3 above 100 MHz, which ADR-0035 rules out
  ("CL2 always": CL3 drew no 3D picture in run G1); GC2 and the soaks run
  133.25 MHz with CL2 and rendered there. Fixed: `clock::Speed::ladder`
  (CL2 at every clock), which G3 now uses. The first run's 133.25 results
  say nothing about the chip. (All 20 timed out at 200 ms, the
  target never written), every way: the cores at 133 MHz don't run at all
  here (GC2 rendered there, wrongly, with the screen on).
- **G3 again, fixed (2026-10-23, `local/rp-glesfeat-G3b.txt`, card log
  `glesfeat-0008`, sha 52270bba): CL2 at every setting (ADR-0035 rule 3)
  and the display checked after every clock change (rule 8, C69). The
  screen stayed on at all 24 settings (no underrun, the signature moving),
  every control right.** **Every way still 0 of 20 right at all three
  settings** (9,000-19,500 grey words in 20 renders), **133.25 / 1 now
  renders** (CL2: the first run's no-render was its CL3), wrong like the
  rest. **The quad's render times with the screen on** (way 1; Windows
  Mobile's 12.95 ms): 74.75 / 1 12.57 ms (1.03x), 99.67 / 1 10.07 ms
  (1.29x), 133.25 / 1 8.38 ms (1.55x). The first run's screen-off times
  were up to 17% faster (8.4 against 10.1 ms at 99.67 / 1): the owner's
  concern was right, the dark display skews speed.
- **Speed, if it were right:** the quad renders in 10.0-10.5 ms at 74.75 / 1
  and 8.2-8.5 ms at 99.67 / 1 against 12.95 ms at Windows Mobile's clock:
  1.26x and 1.55x, against the 1.40-1.50x 149.50 / 2 already gives right.
- Every background drew alone exactly (hash `0xA8005DC5`, the all-black
  picture, the same for all six that cover the screen).

## Dell's colour clear, read from libGLES_CL (static, 2026-10-23)

Read for test G3 (G2 found our flat background object behind the divide-1
Gouraud errors; Dell's probe draws right there after a `glClear`).
`libGLES_CL.dll` (ROM A08, image base `0x0225C000`):

| Finding | Confidence | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `glClearColorx` clamps each channel to 0-1 and packs ARGB8888 (each x 255, truncated; blue in the low byte, alpha the top) into context `+0x5F4` | likely (code) | `0x0225E380`, packer `0x022600D8` |
| `glClear` draws nothing: with `GL_COLOR_BUFFER_BIT` (and the colour mask `+0x5F8` set) it marks a clear pending (`+0x6D8` bit 0) and copies the colour to `+0x6EC`; `GL_DEPTH_BUFFER_BIT` marks bit 1 and the depth `+0x6F0` | likely (code) | `0x0225F064`-`0x0225F0F0` |
| **The next draw sends the pending clear as a real object** (`0x0226E744`): state block `0x10000043` (TSP, ISP, the bit-6 word; no clip word), **TSP `0x22200E80`** (Dell's flat opaque TSP, the same as our background's; `0x22200E00` with a depth clear; bit 8 set when the colour isn't cleared), ISP `0xE0000000`, bit-6 word 0, then **one strip of four 5-word vertices** (x, y, the clear depth, 1/w 1.0, the clear colour) over the viewport rectangle (`+0x6DC`-`+0x6E8`) in the order top left, bottom left, top right, bottom right | likely (code) | `0x0226E744`-`0x0226E8BC` |
| The colourless clear the TA path sent in versions 14-15 and G (`0x0226DFF0`: mask `0x10000053`, TSP `0x22206F80`, x/y/z only, a triangle to 2w x 2h) is another routine, reached from `0x0226E6xx` (a scissored or partial clear, likely), not `glClear`'s colour path | likely (code) | the call at `0x0226E734` |

So under Dell's quad in `rp-wmprobe` (black clear colour, `glClear(GL_COLOR_BUFFER_BIT)`
before every draw) lies **an opaque flat background like ours, but a
screen-sized four-corner quad at the clear depth**, where ours is one
triangle reaching to (2048, 2048) at depth 0. Test G3 tries each
difference.

## Test G2: the background object is what breaks Gouraud at divide 1 (2026-10-23)

**`rp-glesfeat` test G2 on the X51v (`local/rp-glesfeat-G2.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0006`, sha 9d7f2aca): GC2's ARGB8888 Gouraud quad eight ways,
each its own reference at Windows Mobile's clock, 20 renders at 74.75 MHz /
1, a control after (every control right; the clock read back as Windows
Mobile's each time).**

| Way | Background | Right at 74.75 / 1 | Wrong words (20 renders) | Of them inside the picture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1: ours (G's way 2) | our flat triangle | 0 | 16,433 | 16,341 |
| 2: one four-corner object | our flat triangle | 0 | 18,312 | 18,213 |
| 3: z 1.0 | our flat triangle | 0 | 16,253 | 16,160 |
| 4: **the clear underneath** | **the clear's object** | 3 | **24** | **0** |
| 5: per-tile lists | our flat triangle | 0 | 18,209 | 18,076 |
| 6: all four (the TA's form by hand) | the clear's object | 1 | 43 | 0 |
| 7: the TA, a strip of four | the clear's object | 6 | 30 | 0 |
| 8: the TA, two draws | the clear's object | 2 | 42 | 0 |

- **The cause is the background object, not the Gouraud object.** With
  our flat background triangle (TSP `0x22200E80`, list layout 4, 17
  words) under the quad, about 820 Gouraud words a render are wrong at
  divide 1 whatever form the quad takes (two triangles or one four-corner
  object, z 0 or 1, every-tile or per-tile lists). With the GLES clear's
  object underneath (TSP `0x22206F80`, layout 0, 10 words: no colours)
  **not one word inside the picture is wrong in 80 renders** (ways 4, 6,
  7, 8), by our direct path as by the TA.
- **The wrong words with our background are grey:** each one's three
  colour channels equal (`0xFFB9B9B9` for `0xFFB8B7B5`, `0xFF202020` for
  `0xFF1E1E1B`, `0xFF242424` for `0xFF252421`, `0xFF1C1C1C` for `0xFF1C1C19`):
  one channel's value in all three, as if the shading took another
  object's (or another channel's) colour plane. Not one step off: a fault,
  not rounding.
- **What's left with the clear underneath is the clear's own colour:**
  every remaining wrong word is on the left edge (x = 0, rows 109-591),
  where the quad leaves the clear showing (`0x00000000` at Windows Mobile's
  clock) and divide 1 gives `0x48EEB980`. The clear's object carries no
  colour; where its colour comes from (a register Dell's `glClearColor`
  sets, likely) is not yet read. Dell's probe is right there: it sets it.
- **One four-corner object draws exactly what two triangles draw** at
  Windows Mobile's clock (ways 1, 2, 3, 5: one picture, hash `0x4A8221C0`;
  ways 4, 6, 7, 8: hash `0x9DA59DC5`): a quad in 22 words instead of 34, for
  the renderer to take (results, not a decision).
- **The TA's two draws repeated this time** (way 8; G's way 1 differed in
  every word): G's failure didn't recur (cause open; G's was the first TA
  render after the image started).
- Render times: per-tile lists' times include the CPU building 1,200
  lists (about 7.5 ms), not a 3D cost.

## Test G: Gouraud at divide 1, through the TA against our objects (2026-10-23)

**`rp-glesfeat` test G on the X51v (`local/rp-glesfeat-G1.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0005`, sha 91ce5c79): GC2's ARGB8888 Gouraud quad, each way its
own reference at Windows Mobile's clock, 20 renders at 74.75 MHz / 1, a
control at Windows Mobile's clock after (every control right, the clock
read back as Windows Mobile's each time).**

| Way | Reference repeatable | Right at 74.75 / 1 | Wrong words (20 renders) | Worst step |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1: through the TA, each triangle its own draw | **no** (the two renders differ in every word) | not run | - | - |
| 2: our objects (two triangles, layout 4) | yes | **0 of 20** | 16,338 (about 820 a render) | 251 |
| 3: ours with the TA's list flags | yes | 0 of 20 | 16,538 | 250 |
| 4: through the TA, the quad as one strip of four | yes | **5 of 20** | **33** (about 2 a wrong render) | 238 |

- **The layout suspect is ruled out.** For a Gouraud triangle the TA
  writes list words `0x40809840`/`0x40809851`, **layout 4, our flags
  exactly** (way 3 took them and equals way 2), and the object word for
  word in our 17-word form; only z differs (1.0, as sent, against our 0).
- **A strip of four is one object of a new form:** list word
  `0x60A098A0`: mask bits 30 and 29 (two triangles) and **layout 5**;
  the object ISP, TSP, x/y x4, z x4, 1/w x4, colour x4 (22 words). Dell's
  probe draws its quad the same way (`glDrawArrays(GL_TRIANGLE_STRIP, 0,
  4)`), and Dell's quad is right at divide 1 every time.
- **That form is nearly right at divide 1** where ours is wrong in every
  render: 33 wrong words in 20 renders against 16,338. The first wrong
  word is on the quad's left edge (0, 107), where the reference holds the
  clear's `0x00000000` (the GLES clear, a layout-0 object, under the
  quad); whether every one of the 33 is an edge pixel, the clear's rather
  than the quad's, isn't logged.
- **Way 1 never repeated at Windows Mobile's clock** (two renders differ in
  all 307,200 words), so it has no result; its clear's list word pointed
  past the parameter memory (`0xD088D8`), where the 8 MiB reach aliases
  (ADR-0024 M5). Cause open.
- **Render times hardly change at divide 1** with the system clock at 74.75:
  ours 12.95 ms at Windows Mobile's clock, 12.58 at 74.75 / 1; the strip
  16.6 / 17.7 (with the TA). The cores gain 1.5x but the memory loses 25%.
- **What differs between way 2 and way 4** (to isolate): one 4-vertex
  object (layout 5, two-triangle mask) against two 3-vertex objects; z 1.0
  against 0; the clear's layout-0 object against our flat background
  triangle; the TA's per-tile lists (only the objects touching a tile)
  against ours (every object in every tile).

## Dell's own driver draws Gouraud right at divide 1 (rp-wmprobe, Windows Mobile, 2026-10-23)

**`rp-wmprobe` module 1 (`local/rp-wmprobe-1.txt`; the owner ran it under
Windows Mobile 6.1, his community build), two runs and a soft reset:**
run 1 as Windows Mobile runs (`CoreClock` absent: 99.67 MHz, the cores at
divide 2), run 2 with `HKLM\Drivers\Display\PowerVR\CoreClock` =
75,000,000.

- **Dell's driver chose divide 1 itself:** run 2's registers read
  `COREPLL` `0xBC1` (74.75 MHz), `MBXCLK` `0x1` then `0x2` (divider 1),
  **`M24CLK` `0x0`** (its divider 1 too), `LMTIM` `0x62272`, `LMREFRESH`
  `0x490`.
- **A smooth-shaded quad (gc2.rs's corners, GL_SMOOTH, RGB565 surface)
  drawn 20 times through Dell's `libGLES_CL` and read back: all 20
  identical, and identical to run 1's at divide 2.** So the cores draw
  Gouraud right at divide 1 under Dell's driver: **divide 1 works on this
  chip; RetroPane's set-up differs.**
- What Dell's state has that ours didn't at divide 1: **`M24CLK`'s
  divider at 1** (ours stayed at Windows Mobile's 2: run GC2M tests it);
  `SDCLK` 1 after drawing (0 while idle; ours is 1 already under the
  loader); `SYSCLKSRC` 1 while idle (the display PLL) and 2 drawing; the
  render table as GC2F's Dell table (`0x610` `0x30001`, `0x61C`
  `0x20007`, `0x648` 1, `0x6DC` `0x3F800001`, `0x6D8` `0x09000000`; GC2F:
  no effect on the errors).
- Run GC2M built (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2M`, sha 1c00f45c): GC2's nine kinds at
  divide 1 (58.50, 74.75, 99.67, 133.25 MHz) under `M24CLK` as found, 0
  and 1.

## Run GC2S: the screen in the safe region fixes divide 2's overclock, not divide 1 (X51v, 2026-10-24)

**Run GC2S** (`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_SAFE=1`, card log `gpubench-0082`,
`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2S.txt`, image sha 175db7f5): run GC2's rules
(nine kinds, 20 renders a kind a setting, each followed by a control at
Windows Mobile's clock, rising then falling) with its 16-bit label
scanned out from local memory offset 0 (600 KiB, ending at `0x96000`,
inside the region test G19 found safe: quality audit C71) instead of
`rp-vram-map`'s second screen. Every reference repeatable and the same at
the end; every control right (9 of 9 at every setting); no hang; Windows
Mobile's clock put back (`MBXCLK` `0x5` at the exit). Test-only:
`rp-vram-map` is unchanged until the owner rules the placement.

| Setting | Kinds right (of 9) | With the screen outside (runs GC2, GC2H) |
|---|---|---|
| 74.75 / 1 (Dell's) | **7**: the two ARGB8888 Gouraud kinds wrong | 3 |
| 83.20, 91.00, 99.67, 108.33, 117.00, 133.25 / 1 | 3 (the GS scene, the bilinear texture, the blended 4444) | 3 |
| 117.00 / 2 | 9 | 9 |
| **133.25 / 2** (today's overclock) | **9** | 7 |
| **149.50 / 2** | **9** | 7 |

Both directions agree, setting by setting. At divide 1 every Gouraud kind
is wrong in 20 of 20 renders above 74.75 MHz; the wrong words grow with
the clock (RGB565 Gouraud: about 8 a render at 83.20, 14 at 99.67, 40 at
108.33-117.00, 230 at 133.25; ARGB8888 Gouraud 700-1,250), and stay wrong
when read back at Windows Mobile's clock (written wrong, not read wrong).
At 74.75 / 1 the two ARGB8888 Gouraud kinds are wrong by about 1,000
words a render, each drawn grey (its channels equal) where the reference
is slightly tinted, e.g. `0xff1c1c17` drawn `0xff1c1c1c`: test G2's mark
of our flat background object.

Render times (best of 20, ms), the kinds that are right everywhere:

| Kind | 74.75 / 1 | 99.67 / 1 | 117.00 / 1 | 133.25 / 1 | 133.25 / 2 | 149.50 / 2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ARGB8888 GS scene | 26.5 | 20.9 | 18.0 | 16.6 | 26.2 | 23.4 |
| RGB565 bilinear texture | 16.9 | 13.7 | 11.8 | 11.2 | 15.6 | 14.1 |
| RGB565 Gouraud (wrong at / 1 above 74.75) | 8.3 | 6.6 | 5.7 | 5.8 | 9.3 | 8.3 |

With the screen safe, 133.25 / 2's GS scene took 26.2 ms against run
GC2's 27.4 (4% faster) and the bilinear texture 15.6 against 16.5 (5%);
Gouraud the same (9.3 both): C71's scan-out cost, gone.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the scan-out placement was the
whole of divide 2's overclock fault: at 133.25 and 149.50 / 2 every kind
is right once the screen sits in the safe region, where with the screen
outside two ARGB8888 Gouraud kinds lost a few words. At divide 1 it fixes
the RGB565 Gouraud kinds at Dell's 74.75 MHz only; above it, Gouraud
stays wrong exactly as with the screen outside, so divide 1 above 74.75
has a second cause, and G2's (our flat background object) is the
measured candidate: the next divide-1 step combines the safe screen with
G2's colourless background. Divide 1 would pay: 133.25 / 1 renders the
GS scene in 16.6 ms against 23.4 at 149.50 / 2 (29% less), the texture
in 11.2 against 14.1 (21% less). These are the owner's to rule (P1, P2,
V2, V13; ADR-0035).
**Noted for the game ports (the owner, 2026-10-24):** the kinds right at
every divide-1 step are the textured, blended and 32-bit textured ones;
only Gouraud fails. A renderer that lights with textures instead of
shading is an option to measure (apps-and-packs.md section 6).

## Run GC2W: the write-out downsample hides divide 1's errors, it doesn't remove them (X51v, 2026-10-23)

**`rp-gpubench` run GC2W (`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2W-1.txt`, card log
`gpubench-0066`, image a577f0f2, ~70 minutes):** GC2's nine kinds at
58.50, 99.67 and 133.25 / 1, up then down, 20 renders each, under the
plain write-out and `0x644` = 1 (rows averaged), 2 (columns) and 3 (2 x
2), each against its own references at Windows Mobile's clock.

- **The test's own flaw, found here:** with the downsample the core
  writes only half (or a quarter) of the target, and the rest keeps what
  an earlier render left. For the RGB565 and dithered Gouraud kinds that
  stale part changed between renders: their references at the end
  differ from the start by exactly the unwritten part (76,800 words of
  153,600 rows averaged; 38,400 columns; 57,398 both), and their
  controls fail too. Those two kinds say nothing about the clock under
  the downsample. **To fix (quality audit):** compare only the written
  part under `0x644`.
- **The other seven kinds:** under the plain write-out, textures, the GS
  scene and Gouraud + blended 4444 right, the rest wrong (GC2D's
  result). Under the downsample, **ARGB8888 Gouraud still wrong in 20 of
  20 renders** with rows or columns averaged at 99.67 and 133.25, and
  with 2 x 2 at 133.25 (1 of 20 at 99.67); the capped kinds and the
  Gouraud + 8888 texture kind wrong at some settings (RGB565 capped:
  right at 99.67 with rows averaged, wrong in 19 of 20 at 133.25).
- **So the downsample doesn't remove divide 1's errors: averaging hides a
  share of them** (more averaging, fewer left; a faster clock, more
  back). GC2X's clean three renders were at the slowest setting. **Divide
  1 stays wrong under our renderer.** Not a chip limit yet (corrected
  2026-10-23, the owner): Intel lists divide 1 as valid to 75 MHz and
  Dell's driver chooses it for a `CoreClock` at or under 75 MHz; Dell's
  own driver at divide 1 is untested, and the brute force varied
  registers only, not our objects' words.

## Run GC2X: of 426 hidden bits, only the write-out downsample draws right at divide 1 (X51v, 2026-10-23)

**`rp-gpubench` run GC2X (`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2X-1.txt`, card log
`gpubench-0064`, image 6542d8f6; the owner's brute force, point 3, the
owner nearby): 426 flips, each alone at 58.50 / 1 against its own
reference at Windows Mobile's clock, three RGB565 Gouraud renders each,
a control after each.** Every bit of the render table's `0x61C`,
`0x644`, `0x648`, `0x64C`, `0x6D0`, `0x6D8`; the reserved bits of
`MBXCLK`, `SYSCFG`, `SYSCLKSRC`, `VOVRCLK`, `PIXCLK`, `MEMCLK`, `M24CLK`,
`SDCLK` and `COREPLL`.

- **None hung; every control right.**
- **Two drew right at divide 1: `0x644` bit 0 and bit 1**, the write-out
  downsample (rows or columns averaged 2:1 as the picture is written:
  run T2), 0 wrong pixels in 3 of 3 renders each, where divide 1 without
  it had ~590 wrong pixels in 3 of 3. Every other flip: divide 1 as wrong
  as without it.
- Four flips changed the picture at Windows Mobile's clock: `0x61C` bits 0
  and 1 (the rounding mode GC2F saw), `0x644` bits 0 and 1 (the
  downsample).
- What it suggests (to test, run GC2W): the pixels are computed right at
  divide 1 and go wrong in the plain write-out to memory; the averaging
  write-out takes another path. Or the averaging hides small errors (a +1
  in one of two averaged pixels survives truncation about half the time,
  so a hidden 590 should leave ~300: 0 argues against hiding, on 3
  renders).

**`rp-gpubench` run GC2H2 (`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2H2-1.txt`, card log

and 169.00 MHz, the cores at divide 4, 3 and 2, up then down.**

  at 162.50 and 169.00 most renders never finished (1,388 hung renders
  recovered by the renderer's reset).

  wrong after 156.00 (5-6 of 9 right) and after 162.50 and 169.00 (0-1 of
